2026-10-18 P3 pending-start records and explicit recovery scope
-------------------------------------------------------------
Inspected P:nmrpeak_provider/attempt_journal.py and attempt_journal_store.py;
S:src/secs_inference/provider/attempt_state.py; M:magnet_provider/terminal_outbox.py.
P's admit/replace/_replace_record/_require_recovery_space establish the intended
next storage ordering: private bounded reads, expected-record comparison, staged
write, file fsync, atomic replacement and directory fsync, plus retained uncertainty
after failures. Free-space arithmetic is not physical reservation against other
disk writers. M's owner binding separates API/provider identity from record bytes.
Use these behaviors, not a sibling-specific journal schema or sync control loop.

Design review narrowed the first implementation to pending starts rather than
freezing all future lifecycle records before their consumers exist. StartPending
retains exact start body, input identity and expected analysis kind. RecoveryScope
adds canonical API origin, provider, consumer/lane and generation identity; restore
requires an explicit matching scope. No credential, live handle or prepared-input
fingerprint is serialized. Future prepared state is historical evidence only;
repreparation requires explicit source/generation policy and a fresh admitted
handle. Terminal records must preserve execution-entry/retirement facts and keep
request/evidence through confirmed publication. These remain required next slices.

The new bounded codec rejects missing/unknown/duplicate/malformed fields, foreign
scope, noncanonical base64 and body/Job mismatch. Encoding preserves the exact
request, including legal whitespace/order. Reviews found an actual closure defect:
unbounded origin text could encode beyond the 65536-byte decode ceiling. A focused
regression reproduced it; encoding now checks actual serialized size before emitting
bytes. No arbitrary DNS-length rule was added. The redundant state field was also
removed because the pending-start schema already identifies this single state.

Contained installed validation passes 83 unit tests and five contract tests; Ruff
passes. Contract checks establish installed module inventory/import independence.
The final bound correction reran the affected unit lane; unchanged inventory was
not retested. Independent wire/failure/all-U&A reviews found no residual source
findings after correction. No filesystem durability, cancellation ownership, claim
exclusion or restart proof is claimed by this pure codec. Full-sized retention
capacity, owned async file I/O, later lifecycle states, recovery and P4/P5 remain
open under the original goal. The 65536-byte pending-record ceiling must not become
the future terminal retention reservation.

2026-10-18 P3 owned pending-start storage
---------------------------------------
AttemptFiles owns blocking private-directory operations; AttemptStore owns one
submitted worker future on the async control loop. Admission uses bounded reads,
same-key comparison, exclusive staging, file fsync, exclusive publication and
directory fsync. Identical requests preserve the original obligation; conflicting
facts cannot overwrite it. Local flock excludes cooperating processes using the
same directory inode, not other hosts or directory-path substitution. The source
docstring and package README identify P's precise storage precedent and the
intentional difference: retain cleanup failures rather than suppress them.

Cancellation/timeout leaves the original operation owned. New work and close are
refused until explicit settlement; no second write is submitted. Publication failure
preserves staging and any visible target and poisons further use. Visibility does
not imply durability. Reopening validates and syncs the retained inventory; unknown
staging requires explicit reconciliation. Slot/headroom arithmetic covers supplied
full-record budgets, but does not reserve physical disk against unrelated writers.
Full terminal record sizing remains part of the later lifecycle qualification.

Tests first failed for the missing store. Independent audits then found a real U14
defect: only directory-close failure recorded uncertain retirement. A reproduced
regression showed that failed record/staging closure could be hidden by successful
directory closure. All owned close paths now retain sticky uncertainty, never retry
a detached descriptor number, and still attempt remaining directory cleanup.
Primary errors and cleanup evidence remain separately available for protected use.

Focused installed-container validation: 93 unit tests and 22 integration tests
pass. Five installation contracts passed for the new module inventory. Tests cover
short writes, file/directory fsync, link/unlink and write failure, preserved cleanup
evidence, visibility versus durability, duplicate/conflicting keys, headroom refusal,
cancelled/expired waits and settlement of the original write. A separate-process
integration test proves lock exclusion and successful readback after owner closure.
These are local filesystem/runtime witnesses, not power-loss or cross-host proof.

Applicable U07/U08/U11/U13 fault evidence and U14 retirement findings are resolved;
independent wire, failure and full-U/A source reviews found no residual actionable
issue. This slice admits pending starts only. Active/terminal states, replacement,
execution-entry ownership, recovery policy, NMRNet composition, recipient diagnostics
and end-to-end/sibling qualification remain required under the active goal. Final
documentation review corrected stale future-owner wording; the existing file owner
checks composition-supplied capacity. Ruff and the final affected adversarial lane
(73 tests) pass after binding test fault callbacks explicitly.

2026-10-18 P3 acknowledgement and durable execution entry
--------------------------------------------------------
AcknowledgedAttempt nests the original StartPending and admitted start response,
plus LocalExecutionPhase.NOT_ENTERED or EXECUTION_ENTERED. Both server and local
facts survive restart independently. Terminal start replays remain representable
but cannot construct or transition to execution-entered state. Preparation remains
a live, session-owned handle, not a serialized prepared flag. Source/README cite
P:attempt_journal.py::bind_started_attempt/mark_execution_entered and
P:attempt_lifecycle.py::PreparedForExecution at the established pinned revision.
The retained terminal replay is an intentional extension of P's active-only record.

AttemptStore.replace compares the entire expected retained record and permits only
implemented forward transitions. Original start bytes and acknowledgement facts
cannot change. Replacement stages, syncs, atomically replaces and syncs the directory;
one existing slot suffices, with headroom for growth and staged replacement. A failed
replacement preserves the old or installed new record and blocks further work.
Cancellation retains the original worker result for explicit settlement. The read
and minimum-capacity bounds grew together to cover the bounded pending document,
bounded acknowledgement and envelope; pending encoding retains its 65536-byte cap.

Tests first failed for missing transitions. The first implementation exposed a
classification error: invalid pre-effect transitions became StoreFailed through
the async boundary. They now produce StoreRefused before storage mutation. Review
found the same inconsistency for oversized initial admission; its regression also
failed before correction. Neither refusal poisons storage or writes staging data.

Focused contained validation passed 102 unit and 23 integration tests before that
final admission-classification correction. Replacement tests cover staging write,
file sync, atomic replace and post-replace directory-sync failure, original-future
settlement after cancellation, exact headroom limits, stale/foreign/backward refusal,
and a maximum pending record through acknowledgement/entry encoding and decoding.
A child process exits without cleanup after durable entry; the next owner reads
the entered record and cannot mark another entry. This establishes local process
restart retention, not power-loss proof or a complete scientific recovery workflow.

Independent wire, failure and applicable-U/A reviews resolved the replacement fault
evidence gap. NOT_ENTERED remains historical state, never proof of a prepared handle.
This slice does not invoke science: lifecycle claim checks, fresh server observations,
preparation/generation policy and ordering the actual handle call remain required.
Terminal retention/evidence, publication replay, inventory recovery, NMRNet binding,
recipient diagnostics and P5 qualification remain open under the original goal.
The final affected adversarial lane passes all 80 tests after the admission fix;
package Ruff passes. Final independent wire, failure and full applicable-U/A reviews
report no residual actionable source or documentation finding.

2026-10-18 P3 immutable terminal records and required evidence
-------------------------------------------------------------
TerminalPending retains the acknowledged Attempt, COMPLETE/FAIL operation, exact
request and required ProtectedEvidence. TerminalConfirmed adds only a receipt
validated against that exact request. TerminalHeld retains an operation-specific
expiry, opposite-outcome or replay-mismatch conflict and optional request ID. CAS
cannot replace the chosen outcome/evidence, reverse a hold or return to execution.
These are record semantics; the actual claimed publisher/recovery loop is next.

Evidence uses a binding-owned schema and immutable 1..262144 bytes. The ceiling is
grounded in P:nmrpeak_provider/attempt_diagnostic.py::MAX_DIAGNOSTIC_BYTES at the
established pin; its history-trimming behavior is intentionally not copied. Required
evidence is rejected when oversized, never silently shortened. S:execution.py's
record_report-before-publication ordering grounds the evidence-first obligation.
Binding owns necessary causal/retirement facts, content meaning and secret removal.
Core owns exact private retention and bounds, not automatic secret detection.

Review clarified that unresolved worker cleanup must remain retainable: requiring
retirement before saving an outcome could lose primary evidence when cleanup fails.
Receipt and retirement are independent. This slice does not release claims, delete
records or reuse workers. Typed execution identity/retirement authority belongs with
the real preparation/handle owner, not an invented boolean in a byte codec. Opaque
evidence and a matching server receipt must never become resource-release proof.

The maximum retained record derives from bounded start/admission, the full 2 MiB
completion request (including legal whitespace), request/evidence base64 expansion,
schema metadata and a bounded receipt. Read and minimum StoreLimits capacity share
that bound, checking configured headroom for full outcome capacity before initial
admission; physical blocks are not reserved. Actual encoded length is checked.
Request JSON is never normalized; nested records stay JSON objects
rather than recursively base64-expanding the entire envelope.

Tests first failed for missing terminal records. The unit lane then passed 109 tests.
Added composed cases pass all 24 integration tests: maximum completion/failure bodies,
maximum evidence and supplementary-Unicode schema/message text, maximum pending-start
envelope, matching receipt, exact minimum record budget, actual storage and reopen.
The final affected adversarial lane passes 88 tests: foreign/corrupt nested records,
canonical-base64 rejection, wrong receipts/hold metadata, safe rendered tracebacks,
full-capacity admission, immutable outcome/evidence and held-state reopen. Evidence
sync failure preserves the prior acknowledged record and exact staged evidence while
blocking reuse. Package Ruff passes. No dependency/module-inventory change occurred.

Source references/rationale are recorded in attempt_record.py and package README.
Publication send ordering, exact replay after lost acknowledgement, durable receipt
failure, held-conflict dispatch prevention, execution/claim ownership and real recipient
delivery still require orchestration witnesses. No scientific or deployed recovery
claim follows from these storage tests; P3/P4/P5 remain open under the full goal.
Final independent wire, failure and applicable-U/A reviews found no residual source
issue. Documentation review corrected reservation wording to describe headroom checks.

P3 publication slice (2026-10-18)
-------------------------------------------------------------------
TerminalPublisher now observes the exact durable pending record before sending,
then retains the matching receipt or reviewed operation-specific conflict. It
never retries implicitly, changes the original outcome, or releases worker/lifecycle
ownership. Actual TLS reply loss plus store close/reopen proves explicit unchanged
resend and durable confirmation; the previous pending record cannot be sent again.

AttemptClaims shares one local registry across lanes/recovery, keyed by API origin,
provider and remote Attempt. Consumer/generation labels cannot partition a remote
Attempt's ownership. The precedent is Magnet revision M's
magnet_provider/provider_attempt_recovery.py::ProviderAttemptOwnership; exact replay
follows P's nmrpeak_provider/attempt_journal.py::prepared_terminal_replay and required
report retention follows S's src/secs_inference/provider/execution.py::_publish
(pins above and source/README; paths and symbols checked against pinned Git trees). This is local
exclusion, not distributed locking or proof that a scientific worker has stopped.

Independent review caught two concrete defects and both received regressions:
- Settlement previously retired whichever API exchange was active. Transport now
  records its admitted caller and refuses foreign retirement; publisher settlement
  touches only its own network/storage operations. Foreign activity neither loses
  its result nor pins this publisher's claim after its own work has settled.
- Cancelled settlement previously replaced the original publication failure.
  Protected evidence now preserves the original cause and ordered subsequent
  settlement failures/cancellations, while CancelledError still propagates.

Tests-first witnesses failed before the fixes. Measured contained lanes: 123 unit,
25 integration and 5 installation-contract tests pass; the final affected

P3 durable start slice (2026-10-18)
---------------------------------
AttemptStarter now owns initial pending retention or explicit existing-record
replay, one exact signed start, synchronous reference binding, and durable server
acknowledgement. It returns only the retained AcknowledgedAttempt; execution remains
NOT_ENTERED even for a replay. Terminal start acknowledgements are preserved too.
No preparation callback, execution permit or worker-release grant is introduced.

Before a remote reference exists, AttemptClaims.claim_start excludes the provider
attempt key across Jobs, lanes and generations. Binding adds the received remote
reference to that same handle without releasing the key; explicit release removes
both. A conflicting remote owner preserves its authority, while the starter retains
the received admission privately and remains pinned for reconciliation. Recovery
of already acknowledged records must acquire both identities in the forthcoming
lifecycle owner; remote-only claim() is not a substitute for pending-key exclusion.

Sources, checked in the pinned Git trees and linked in package README:
- I:nmr_api/operations/execution_attempt_start.py:396-412 reserves provider keys
  across Jobs. Key ownership therefore excludes Job/lane/generation partitions.
- P:nmrpeak_provider/attempt_lifecycle.py:442-508 retains initial admission;
  :648-695 consumes the retained start and binds server acknowledgement;
  :1636-1664 checks generation before pending-start replay. Review corrected the
  earlier attribution of retention to :648-695 alone.

Start and terminal publication keep separate owners: start binds two identities,
whereas publication classifies terminal conflicts. Review rejected a generic
transaction framework as unnecessary. Both use exact owner tags for asynchronous
storage and API settlement. Initial-retention settlement sends nothing and returns
no acknowledgement; interrupted acknowledgement replacement returns only its
original completed write. Neither path silently repeats work. Failed replacement
or alias binding preserves protected admission and primary cause; settlement adds
secondary failures without replacing the primary. Reconciliation remains explicit.

Tests first failed for absent start ownership and starting module. The first unit
run then passed 126 tests. Added adverse witnesses pass 104 adversarial tests:
initial-retention failure sends nothing; cancellation before start preserves foreign
API activity; acknowledgement-write failure/cancellation retains identity and claims;
reference conflict preserves both owners; stale/absent/wrong-generation recovery
sends nothing; same key across Jobs/lanes remains exclusive. The 26 integration tests
include real TLS response loss, original pending retention, store close/reopen,
explicit byte-identical replay and durable acknowledgement, with no third stale send.
Five installation contracts and package Ruff pass. These are contained client-side
proofs with scripted peers, not deployed server idempotency or scientific execution.

Independent source/failure/wire and applicable-U/A reviews found no behavioral
finding after the source-reference correction. Relevant dispositions: U05/U07-U11/
U14/U15/U17/U18/U22/U23/U25/U26 and A01/A03-A05/A10; science, deployment, dependency
and Make policies remain unchanged. Remaining full-goal work: claimed recovery of
later states and inventory candidates, preparation/live handles, progress and durable
entry ordering, actual retirement authority, recipient diagnostics, NMRNet binding,
sibling reuse and complete 003 qualification. P3/P4/P5 remain open.

P3/P4 scientific preparation boundary (2026-10-18)
-------------------------------------------------
Source inspection before connecting core execution found a real integration gap:
prepare_direct_job supplies geometry, but dictionary admission and tensor-derived
input rejection still occurred inside the direct inference path. A prepared-data
record is not a live execution handle. Existing inference_native retirement also
explicitly covers local client processes only, not the scientific container.

PredictionSession now provides the synchronous scientific side of that boundary.
It parses and dictionary-admits the request, then composes every required H/C tensor
before returning a private, opaque current handle. This includes centered-coordinate
and pairwise-distance finite checks, not only syntactic/dictionary admission.
Replacing preparation invalidates the old handle even if the replacement fails.
Execution accepts only the current object, consumes it before the first model load,
and cannot be retried after a partial head or loader failure. Prepared tensors and
dictionary remain private. A serial isolated worker must own this session; there is
no claim of thread safety, asynchronous cancellation or engine-side retirement.

Identity precedent verified in pinned P:nmrpeak_provider/runner_session.py:333-350,
generate: current request object and state are checked before entering generation.
The link/rationale appear in README and predict.py. NMRNet model/scaler calculation
was extracted unchanged into predict_composed_element; predict_element calls it
after composition, while the new session calls it with already admitted tensors.
The direct predict_request entrypoint preserves its existing ordering and behavior,
sharing source admission and result aggregation. Scientific units, selected release,
head selection, atom ordering, scaling and numerical tolerances are unchanged.
Both prepared tensor sets now coexist (bounded by the existing 128-atom limit);
model/scaler references still remain scoped to one H/C head at a time.

Tests-first session tests failed for the absent PredictionSession, then passed.
Measured contained lanes: test/unit/prediction-input passes 37 tests;
prediction/tensor-parity passes 26 tests. New cases prove invalid/stale/foreign/None
handles cannot execute, rejection revokes old preparation, model failure consumes
execution, later-head failure returns no partial result, real tensor overflow fails
before model loading, and execution does not recompose input. A deterministic real
Torch forward and scaler give exactly equal direct/session outputs. This is synthetic
model composition evidence, not qualification of the new session with released weights.
Existing reference tensor comparisons still pass. No upstream or sibling code changed.

Runtime-source Ruff passes. Direct Ruff over the two legacy test files reports
29 pre-existing findings; the same 29 were confirmed against exact HEAD copies.
Unrelated formatter changes were removed, leaving the new tests and intended source
edits only. Independent failure, scientific/mechanical and applicable-U/A reviews
found no actionable behavior defect; reviews explicitly corrected the tensor-lifetime
and scientific qualification descriptions. U01-U05/U07-U11/U13-U19/U21-U23/U25/U26
and A01/A03/A05 remain distinguished from unexecuted deployment/science claims.

Next integration remains required: a real asynchronous worker/controller owning
exact execution identity and confirmed container/grant retirement; core preparation,
progress and durable execution-entry ordering; subsequent recovery and recipient
projections. The session alone does not make provider-core or 003 complete.

P3 execution orchestration boundary (2026-10-18)
-----------------------------------------------
AttemptExecutor now connects retained admission, verified input, preparation,
execution entry and terminal retention under one continuous lifecycle claim.
AnalysisSession names the binding-owned asynchronous preparation/execution/retirement
contract; core does not manufacture or serialize its live handle. Session ownership
transfers only after local admission and claim acquisition. Preparation failures
classified by the binding may produce a retained failure without entering science.
Unexpected binding or encoding exceptions retain their original cause instead.

Source: P:nmrpeak_provider/attempt_lifecycle.py::execute_prepared, lines 1130-1240,
verified at 1697dc84dd1d76ae25b04efc8183fd80614000e5 (README links exact revision).
Confirmed running progress precedes durable execution entry; fresh server readback
then precedes handle invocation. Readback is a snapshot, not atomic exclusion against
later cancellation. The outcome is held before encoding/persistence, so failure cannot
silently replace it or authorize another execution. TerminalPending and its required
evidence are retained before retirement and subsequent TerminalPublisher handoff.

Retirement must confirm stopped work and released grants. Python cancellation is not
that proof. Failure paths attempt retirement; a failed normal retirement is not retried
implicitly while handling its own exception. Explicit settlement attempts independent
session/API/storage cleanup even when another cleanup fails, retaining primary and
ordered secondary evidence. It never resumes preparation or invokes science. Claims
remain pinned for uncertain writes, unretained outcomes or unconfirmed retirement.

Tests first failed for the absent execution modules. Contained focused lanes then
passed: normative 30, adversarial 112, integration 27, installation contracts 5.
Package Ruff passes. New adverse cases cover failed running confirmation, oversized
observed outcomes, cancellation during entry/terminal writes, stopped Job readback,
failed terminal retention and failed retirement. An independent review found that a
known StoreRefused incorrectly left prospective write uncertainty: a regression failed
before correction, then passed. Unretained outcomes remain a separate obligation.
Repeated independent failure, wire and applicable-U/A reviews found no remaining
behavior defect in this slice; broader missing lifecycle behavior remains listed below.

The new TLS integration performs start -> prepare -> execute -> terminal publication
using the real client/store and a recording session. It observes seven requests and
retained TerminalConfirmed under one claim. This proves composition and ordering;
it does not qualify real NMRNet execution, worker retirement or deployed API behavior.

Remaining P3/P4/P5: observe stop/expiry while preparation or execution is pending;
implement actual asynchronous NMRNet worker/controller ownership and bounded,
authoritative container/grant retirement; recover later records with both claim
identities; project recipient diagnostics; qualify sibling reuse and full 003 paths.
The package and full provider remain incomplete.

P3 observation during preparation and execution (2026-10-18)
------------------------------------------------------------
AttemptExecutor now owns the original asynchronous task for each session phase.
While it remains pending, sequential owner-tagged reads check Attempt/Job state.
A final read admits successful returns; an already failed binding needs no further
acceptance read. No watcher, scheduler, task replacement or automatic science retry
was introduced. Explicit positive finite poll_seconds controls cadence, while each
read retains the API exchange deadline. Session I/O must use a separate transport.

Precedent verified at P 1697dc84dd1d76ae25b04efc8183fd80614000e5:
nmrpeak_provider/attempt_lifecycle.py::execute_prepared, lines 1287-1340,
polls generation and observes again before accepting candidates. README links the
exact source. Preparation monitoring here extends that precedent. The scientific
session still owns aggregate operation deadlines; polling does not bound science.

A stop, failed poll or caller cancellation requests task cancellation only once.
Session retirement and bounded task_settle_seconds observation are independent:
either unconfirmed condition pins ownership. Settlement observes the same task,
never resumes preparation or executes a handle. Task exceptions are retrieved to
avoid leaking private causes through event-loop warnings; original and secondary
failures remain available through protected ExecutionFailed evidence.

The task captures an execution result or classified preparation failure before any
acceptance read. A result arriving during a failed read or after initial cleanup
times out updates the protected failure evidence and keeps the claim held. It is
not replaced by a fabricated scientific failure or automatically published after
stop. This is in-memory retention, not crash-durable suppressed-result recovery;
that durable reconciliation remains required in the full lifecycle work.

Tests-first observation cases failed on absent executor timing arguments before
implementation. Independent review then found a failure-ordering issue: a newly
started acceptance read could supersede an already completed binding exception.
Retrieving that exception before any acceptance read fixes it; regressions cover
both preparation and execution. Additional witnesses cover stop during each phase,
a cancellation-resistant task with confirmed retirement, repeated settlement with
no second cancellation, final stop after completed science, result during failed
in-flight poll, late execution result after cleanup timeout, and caller cancellation
during a pending owned read. These are recording-port race proofs; actual scientific
container retirement remains unqualified. TLS lifecycle composition now performs
nine requests including the two final acceptance reads.

Measured contained lanes: normative 30, adversarial 119, integration 27; package
Ruff and diff whitespace checks pass. Independent failure, applicable-U/A and wire
reviews converged with no residual actionable finding after the exception-ordering
correction and race witnesses. Relevant U07/U08/U13/U14 ownership and transparency
claims are limited to these observations. P3 still needs delayed-persistence stop
handling and durable recovery/reconciliation; P4/P5 still need actual worker/binding,
recipient diagnostics and full scientific/sibling qualification. Goal remains open.

P3 retained-request recovery and inventory ownership (2026-10-18)
----------------------------------------------------------------
AttemptClaims.claim_records now reserves all start-key and known remote aliases of
a selected inventory atomically. Any existing owner or repeated identity refuses
the complete selection before installing claims. claim_record provides the same
behavior for one record. API origin and provider identify the ownership domain;
consumer/lane and generation do not partition it. Storage still requires its
explicitly opened recovery scope. The shared record
accessor acknowledged_attempt exposes the existing admission without copying facts.

AttemptRecovery owns one selection: StartPending delegates exact resume to the
existing starter; TerminalPending delegates exact publication; acknowledged state
only yields identity-bound readback with its unchanged local execution phase;
confirmed/held state returns unchanged without API I/O. Each path checks current
durable state. Child operations retain their own owner tags and settlement. Recovery
never resumes a scientific handle, synthesizes an interrupted failure, deletes an
obligation or releases the lifecycle claim. Selection/reservation is not proof that
an old controller or worker stopped. Composition must exclude its mutation authority
before replay and separately confirm worker/grant cleanup before reuse.

Source verified from P 1697dc84dd1d76ae25b04efc8183fd80614000e5:
nmrpeak_provider/attempt_journal.py:534-579, decide_restart/_terminal_restart.
The precedent distinguishes exact start/terminal replay and bound observation;
its consumer-specific preparation resumption, interrupted failure and deletion
policies are deliberately not copied into core. README links the precise lines.
Existing publisher conflict/receipt handling remains the single replay authority.

Tests first failed on the missing recovery module. New cases cover atomic rollback
on existing or duplicate remote identities across inventory, stale acknowledgement
zero-send, entered-state readback without science, confirmed/held zero-send, and
cancelled original inventory/readback settlement. Independent review found cancelled
readback dropped current API diagnostic evidence: the fix captures it only around
the actual API await, preserving it through settlement while excluding stale API
failure during inventory cancellation. Paired regressions verify that distinction.
The acknowledgement-write cancellation witness now runs through recovery and proves
original-child settlement, one write and no resend. Existing lost-reply TLS tests now
reopen storage, reserve the inventory, and replay exact start/terminal bytes through
AttemptRecovery; this qualifies the dispatcher with real client/storage effects.

Measured contained qualification: normative 32, adversarial 125, integration 27,
installation contracts 5. These establish retained-request recovery and observation,
not complete lifecycle recovery. Suppressed/in-memory outcomes, delayed-persistence
stop handling, actual worker retirement, NMRNet composition/recipient diagnostics
and full sibling/scientific workflow qualification remain required. No upstream or
sibling code changed. Independent failure and wire rereviews found no residual
behavior defect after the diagnostic correction; applicable-U/A closure is limited
to the exercised ownership/replay/evidence boundaries.
Final documentation review corrected the ownership-domain wording: API/provider
remain part of exclusion identity; consumer/lane/generation do not split it.
Package Ruff and diff whitespace checks pass.

P4 first scientific worker transport (2026-10-18)
------------------------------------------------
Added a private, single-connection transport around the scientific preparation/
execution seam: asynchronous PredictionClient and serial serve_connection. The
worker retains the actual prepared object; a random 32-hex wire token only correlates
that preparation on the admitted allocation/connection. Client handles remain local
objects, consumed before execution. Replacement preparation revokes previous handles,
including locally rejected replacement. There is no reconnect, retry, multiplexing,
queue, warm-worker policy or worker-issued retirement acknowledgement.

Source verified at S 34f5915c1534278bbf61b4c6592afa44cf94ab08:
src/secs_inference/provider/worker.py::_send/_receive/_exact, lines 348-374,
uses four-byte network-order length framing with a 2 MiB ceiling. Its _child_loop
at line 298 separates scientific work from supervisor authority. README links the
framing source. This implementation adds strict duplicate/nonfinite JSON rejection,
exact fields and allocation correlation, and asynchronous bounded controller I/O;
it does not import SECS's warm-worker supervisor or synchronous controller waits.

Exact request bytes are base64 transported after the existing 1 MiB scientific
input bound is checked. Frame prefixes are admitted before payload reads. Aggregate
client deadlines include explicit elapsed checks before dispatch and after decoding,
so buffered/non-yielding completion cannot hand off a late result. Worker I/O uses
bounded whole-frame reads and sendall timeouts; synchronous science requires external
termination authority. Full worker exception chains travel only as private diagnostic
payloads. Frame overflow refuses a diagnostic rather than truncating it; the eventual
host owner must retain stderr for abnormal exits and failed diagnostic transport.

The existing contained test/unit/provider lane now tests the transport. The first
run failed because worker modules were absent. Real process/socket witnesses use
recording science; the child explicitly closes the inherited controller endpoint,
so socket closure is not distorted by forked descriptor ownership. Cancellation
closes the client while the blocked worker remains alive until the fixture releases
it. Other witnesses cover maximal admitted request bytes, fragmented/truncated/
oversized frames, wrong allocations, stale wire tokens, repeated/foreign handles,
private diagnostic rendering, partial reply timeout, explicit-close/late-reply races,
nonfinite JSON, pre-encoding size admission and buffered deadline expiry.

Independent review found and corrected: result-send failure could attempt another
frame on a corrupted stream; request size was checked after encoding; buffered
completion could miss elapsed timeout; rejected local replacement left an older
handle executable. Regression tests cover each. One maximal-input test initially
failed because its synthetic hex result itself exceeded 2 MiB; the corrected fixture
checks received bytes directly with a small result, preserving both real limits.

This is a transport library, not a deployed worker binding. Allocation/connection
identity does not yet bind Attempt/generation; actual PredictionSession/model loading
through this transport, host-owned exact-container/grant retirement, private stderr
retention and controller/API composition remain required. No worker stop receipt or
retirement claim is fabricated. The recording-process tests prove neither model
accuracy nor credential isolation. Existing direct inference is unchanged.

Measured qualification: test/unit/provider passes 37 tests in the existing offline
container lane. Focused Ruff and whitespace checks pass. Repeated independent
failure, wire and applicable-U/A reviews found no residual actionable finding after
the corrections. No image, dependency, upstream or sibling change was required.
The full package/provider goal remains open; this does not close P4/P5.

------------------------------------------------
serve_released_connection now composes the existing SelectedReleaseModels and
PredictionSession with serve_connection. Allocation paths are local configuration,

Added prediction/worker-parity through the existing baseline.sh launcher, sharing

there are no engine sockets, credentials or writable host outputs. The launcher
regression compares exact mount sets and inherited flags, selects the intended test,
and verifies missing binding source refuses admission. Make help and the existing
prediction/qualify aggregate include the new hierarchical lane.

Tests first failed for the missing lane and launcher action. The launcher lane then
passed 18 tests. The scientific test authenticates the same four retained source
records through test_prediction_release.reference_requests and the pinned reference
packet hash. It sends identical label-free bytes through direct inference and the
asynchronous client to a fresh spawned real worker, loading models sequentially to
respect the existing budget. It compares complete ordered results exactly and all
64 original reference positions independently, with zero absolute/relative tolerance.
Worker graceful exit and bounded joining are required; forced cleanup fails the
qualification. These are spawned-process facts, not container-retirement proof.

The emitted NMRNET_WORKER_OBSERVATION record retains mounted source hashes, immutable
image identity, selected release member identities, request/result hashes, observed

agreement in the pinned runtime, not compatibility with arbitrary library versions.

Independent scientific/failure and applicable-U/A reviews found no new behavioral

Attempt/generation binding, separate worker-container authority and host-confirmed

qualification remain required. P4/P5 and the full goal remain open.

P4 exact-container retirement primitive (2026-10-18)
--------------------------------------------------
Added source-only tools/worker_retirement.sh. The host allocation owner supplies its
admitted full CID, private worker.cid receipt/directory and scrubbed engine argv.
The helper refuses entry after unconfirmed local-client retirement, checks the CID
receipt byte-for-byte, and exclusively creates a retirement receipt directory.
It stops that ID once, admits an exact exited/no-PID/not-restarting observation,
then removes that ID without force. Only successful removal returns success.
Each engine call has a ten-second local-client budget; stop has a five-second
engine grace. These are local policy choices, not scientific execution deadlines.

Provenance: tools/docker.sh at parent 362c7c2 supplies the byte-exact CID and bounded
metadata precedent; tools/inference_native.sh at that same revision supplies the
local-client owner. The existing source links there retain the underlying Magnet
precedents. We do not source docker.sh because it is an executable acquisition
launcher. The new helper composes the existing native owner without claiming that
its client-process termination establishes engine-side termination.

Failure/cancellation stops the sequence, retains private receipts and does not
overwrite them on a later invocation. A failed stop may have committed; this
primitive deliberately leaves reconciliation to the allocation owner rather than
retrying. Engine diagnostics remain in private bounded files; controlled public
text names the failed operation, status/cancellation and receipt location.
Review identified that zero exit alone cannot prove complete capture: both files
must now be regular, nonsymlink and strictly below 4096 bytes before advancing.

Tests first failed without the helper. test/unit/provider now passes 45 tests,
including eight retirement tests using a recording engine inside the offline
container. Witnesses cover exact-ID ordering, malformed/still-running observations,
nonzero PID/restart, extra/NUL/oversized bytes, failed stop/inspect/remove, cancellation
during each command, refusal to overwrite uncertain work, and zero-success at-cap
diagnostic capture. Focused Ruff, Bash syntax and whitespace checks pass. Repeated
independent failure, mechanical and applicable-U/A audits found no residual issue
after capture-completeness and cancellation witnesses were added.

This is not yet a live-engine or complete retirement qualification. Exclusive
allocation ownership and create admission are caller preconditions; spelling an ID
is not ownership. Host supervisor integration, separate controller/worker grants,
private scientific stderr retention, grant release and owner-issued acknowledgement
remain required before AnalysisSession.retire can use this result. No production
entrypoint is enabled by this helper alone. P4/P5 and the full goal remain open.

P4 live host-retirement qualification (2026-10-18)
------------------------------------------------
Added make test/integration/provider-worker, an executable consumer of the retirement
primitive. tools/worker_qualification.sh owns the scrubbed Docker client, exact
create/CID admission, detached startup, private files and all engine operations.
tests/integration/provider_worker/retirement_probe.py runs only inside the existing
pinned Python image. The lane inspects the locally present digest reference and
uses its immutable image ID; it cannot pull images or access the network.

Source/rationale: Docker-client and local-process ownership reuse their existing
tools directly. Create admission follows tools/docker.sh at
e845c5a797082ac4fd8d840a1b656847e868d7a1: full CID and complete stdout must agree.
worker_retirement.sh owns the actual stop/inspect/remove sequence. Scientific
baseline image/artifact admission is deliberately not extracted into this probe:
the boundary being measured is engine retirement, independently of model loading.
The preceding real-model worker-parity evidence remains separate.

The worker has its own IPC and capped diagnostics mounts. It sets TERM to ignored,
publishes readiness, then blocks. The host independently observes that exact CID
running before starting the contained controller. The controller can request only
retirement of this allocation; it has no engine socket, CID receipt or owner files.
The host's response directory is mounted read-only into the controller, and an
attempted write is required to fail with EROFS. The worker has no control mount.
Neither probe receives credentials. This is a concrete grant inventory and receipt
write-denial observation, not a general hostile-code isolation claim.

Three fixed live cases now pass:
  unstarted: create an admitted container without starting it, then remove it;
  success: stop/remove the blocked worker, remove its IPC files/directory, then
    atomically publish the allocation-bound retired receipt accepted by controller;
  grant-failure: after worker removal, a fixture-owned retained entry makes actual
    rmdir fail. The host publishes unconfirmed, never retired, and the controller
    requires that disposition. Fixture residue is removed only after both endpoints
    have retired. This fault injection is not a production configuration option.

Every endpoint uses network none, nonroot UID/GID, read-only root, dropped caps,
no-new-privileges, init, no restart, 1 CPU/128 MiB/no swap/32 PIDs and 8 MiB noexec
temporary space. Probe stdout/stderr have separate 64 KiB file limits, with no
daemon log driver; engine metadata uses the existing strict sub-4096-byte receipts.
Successful qualification removes private allocations and Docker client state before
reporting success. Unexpected failure preserves allocation evidence; ambiguous
create/retirement cannot cause automatic replay or deletion by name. General startup
reconciliation and recovery of an unadmitted CID remain outside this fixed probe.

Review exposed a real startup-cleanup omission: a container whose start never ran
can be in created, not exited. Retirement now accepts exact created false 0 false
as well as exact exited false 0 false, with recording-engine and live evidence;
running/restarting/nonzero-PID or malformed observations remain rejected. Review also
corrected final housekeeping so it cannot replace the original failure status or
print qualification success before cleanup finishes.

Final focused validation: test/unit/provider passes 46 tests (2.385 seconds);
test/integration/provider-worker passes all three real-engine cases. Focused Ruff,
Bash syntax and whitespace checks pass. Independent failure, mechanical and
applicable-U/A reviews found no residual actionable issue after revision.

This closes the missing live proof for the fixed retirement primitive and disjoint
host/controller confirmation channel. It does not implement the production
supervisor, AnalysisSession binding, scientific stderr ownership, durable restart
reconciliation, API/interpreter composition or full P4/P5 qualification. These
remain part of the unchanged package goal. No upstream or sibling was edited.

P4 worker-observed scientific provenance (2026-10-18)
--------------------------------------------------
The complete result binding needs evidence from the scientific owner, not a copy
of the controller's selected-model configuration. SelectedReleaseModels now exposes
independent snapshots of the decoded dictionary and successfully loaded model/scaler
pairs. It records identities already compared with the actual decoder input bytes;
it does not reopen artifacts. Unloaded heads and pairs whose scaler decoding failed
are absent. Observations accumulate over one loader lifetime; each released worker
creates its own loader, preventing cross-execution attribution.

ReleasedPredictionSession wraps the existing PredictionSession without changing
tensor composition, model/scaler decoding or prediction calculation. Its private
worker result contains prediction, provenance and private_warnings. Runtime Python
and installed torch/numpy/scikit-learn/joblib distribution versions are collected
during preparation, before model execution. Missing metadata fails preparation
without loading models. Installed metadata is explicitly not an imported-code or
image-identity attestation; host composition still owns runtime/source admission.

The scaler decoder's actual warning emissions are retained on the private worker
channel and forwarded through the original warning hook. Filters remain untouched:
ignored warnings remain ignored, errors still raise, and default-policy repetitions
remain suppressed. A first implementation used catch_warnings; review and a focused
regression showed its filter-version changes could re-emit a previously suppressed
warning. The final serial-worker hook uses try/finally restoration without changing
filter state. Raw messages and source paths are not approved receiver/public text.
The existing bounded frame still rejects overflow rather than truncating evidence.
This hook is for the documented serial scientific worker, not concurrent loaders.

Provenance/rationale: nmrnet_inference/release.py remains the byte-verification owner
introduced for 002; load_scaler_from_bytes retains its exact upstream decoder link
at NMRNetplusplus 3ba8b9c2f3d0a390d00685d238618345c6ab24d7. The composition adapts
those existing owners to note003's result-reproduction requirements, rather than
introducing a second model declaration or trusting qualification fixtures at runtime.
The private transport's existing result field carries this internal envelope; core
does not interpret it and no public result schema is claimed by this slice.

Tests first failed for missing observed-artifact access. Final lightweight
test/unit/prediction-input passes 43 tests in 0.100 seconds, including successful
pair observations, failed scaler exclusion, snapshot independence, warning emission,
ignore/error/default policies, hook restoration and missing runtime metadata.
prediction/worker-parity passes two tests in 41.496 seconds. All 137 predictions
across the four release fixtures remain exactly equal to direct prediction; all 64
historical reference positions remain equal. Returned member identities match the
verified declaration, and the actual 1.5.2-to-1.8.0 scaler warning is retained. The
context-only fixture returns empty predictions and element observations while
retaining the dictionary identity and no scaler warnings.

Refreshed artifacts/prediction-worker-cpu-float32.json captures the final source,
per-fixture worker observations and only warning category names; artifacts/SOURCES.md
records the evidence scope. All 17 recorded source hashes were checked against the
files after capture in an offline container. Repeated independent failure,
mechanical and applicable-U/A audits found no residual issue after revision.

P4 one-geometry scientific result contract (2026-10-18)
----------------------------------------------------
nmrnet_provider/result.py now owns nmrnet.coordinate_shifts.result.v1. A ResultPlan
freezes the complete invariant envelope before inference: binding-admitted
Job/Attempt/input identities, host execution identity, model selection and runtime
expectations; original source record/positions; preparation method and declared-unit
conversion; prepared coordinates and separately labelled float32-admitted values;
exact executed-request digest; scientific limits. Model centering/padding occurs
later and is not misrepresented as those coordinate arrays. Original whitespace is
not needed for reproduction, but its exact request identity remains retained.

This is one geometry inside an ordered geometries collection, not enabled batch
execution. Supported preparation labels describe the existing direct-JSON, table
and XYZ readers. No invented RDKit producer/seed fields or arbitrary preparation
metadata were added; the generated-geometry route must supply its own real variant.
The binding still has to authenticate/configure the supplied identity facts and
retain this plan with its exact live worker handle. Planning grants no execution,
retirement, publication or replay authority.

The released worker now includes the SHA-256 of its admitted request bytes.
Completion requires this exact digest, exact observed dictionary/required head pairs
and runtime metadata, ppm, and one finite numeric prediction at every eligible H/C
position in original order. Missing/extra/reordered predictions, element changes,
boolean indices, nonfinite/unrepresentable numbers and provenance substitutions
refuse the whole result. Empty eligible output requires no loaded heads. Model
selection is labelled separately from per-geometry loaded elements.

Only the known InconsistentVersionWarning receives fixed safe receiver wording.
Raw warning messages, paths and line numbers remain with the private worker outcome;
unclassified warnings prevent publication pending review, rather than disappearing
or entering public text. This is projection policy, not a claim that all future
warnings are harmless. The caller remains responsible for protected evidence and
recipient-specific failure delivery.

The pre-execution capacity calculation serializes the complete invariant envelope
with all punctuation/escaping, the admitted warning and a conservative quoted
32-character allowance per finite binary64 prediction. The final normalized numeric
spelling fits that allowance. Plans above 786432 bytes fail before model work;
completion rechecks the actual result against its reservation without truncation.
That ceiling comes from the pinned API schema documented immediately above. Core's
existing complete_body independently enforces the same opaque-byte bound; this
module does not replace API admission. GUI preview/download qualification remains
required and is separate from storage capacity.

Tests first failed for the missing planner. Final test/unit/provider passes 55 tests
in 2.432 seconds. New tests cover immutable snapshots, coordinate rounding/mapping,
missing/duplicate/reordered/wrong-element rows, integer overflow and inexact binary64
integers, boolean provenance substitutions, private-text canaries, unknown warnings,
empty eligible output and pre-execution size refusal. A 128-eligible-atom fixture
uses extreme finite/subnormal shifts and an admitted warning with escape-heavy
metadata less than one escape unit below the API ceiling; actual bytes fit, and
one extra unit rejects planning. These synthetic limits are not model-capacity proof.

Real prediction/worker-parity now builds each plan before any model run and uses
the actual returned worker evidence to assemble the final result. Both tests pass
in 43.391 seconds: all 137 predictions and 64 historical values remain exactly equal,
and context-only input still produces no predictions. The four final envelopes are
13703, 12757, 13324 and 10662 bytes against reservations 14380, 13334, 13964 and 11023.
No source coordinates were published in the qualification artifact. Its retained
scientific_result fields contain size/digest/reservation; all 19 executed-source
hashes were checked after capture. Final artifact SHA-256:
48b5e734372ff95ce728a217b24ef4b7b2d1258539335f51e2b7f19ca62f1345.

Focused changed-source Ruff and whitespace checks pass. Repeated independent
failure, mechanical and applicable-U/A audits found no residual actionable issue;
the requested near-ceiling capacity witness was added. The first real run passed;
the final run followed the explicit strict-zip lint correction so recorded source
hashes match the committed implementation. No model arithmetic or tolerance changed.

The production AnalysisSession/host-owner composition, full interpreted/generated/
uploaded routes, durable publication through that binding, recipient rendering,

real-worker result consumer, not completion of P4/P5 or the package goal.

Host retirement adapter -- contained controller connection
---------------------------------------------------------
The controller now uses nmrnet_provider/retirement.py in the existing real-engine
qualification. HostRetirement publishes one allocation-specific request and returns
only after the host's exact confirmation and completed local receipt I/O. Its
protocol source is tools/worker_qualification.sh at 1de1074; the host still owns
exact-CID retirement and grant cleanup. The controller receives only its request
mount and the read-only receipt mount, never engine access. The qualifier adds two
exact read-only Python source mounts and uses an allocation name admitted by the
existing worker protocol.

Cancellation and timeout retain the original filesystem task. Repeated calls await
that task; only a completed failed observation permits another read, never another
request. Failed publication refuses adoption even if matching host evidence later
exists. Failed descriptor cleanup is similarly retained for recovery. Filesystem
causes remain chained privately behind safe caller text. Receipt reads reject
symlinks, nonregular files and malformed/wrong-allocation bytes. This does not claim
that a timeout stops a blocked filesystem operation or that socket closure retires
scientific work.

Focused contained validation: test/unit/provider passes 63 tests in 2.500 seconds;
test/integration/provider-worker passes unstarted, success and grant-failure against
real containers with the actual adapter. Focused changed-source Ruff and whitespace
checks pass. Independent mechanical, failure and applicable-U/A reviews found a
post-open private-error escape and unresolved-close gap; both were corrected with
regressions. Subsequent failure and mechanical review found no residual issue.
The initial adapter existed before this turn's tests; tests preceded subsequent
corrections and integration, so this is not claimed as wholly tests-first work.

This closes the contained controller's stop-confirmation connection, not the
production AnalysisSession, API publication or complete package qualification.

Warning evidence correction -- no implicit scientific veto
---------------------------------------------------------
This supersedes the earlier blanket refusal of unclassified warnings. A valid
warning does not itself establish invalid predictions. Decoder exceptions and
existing structural/numerical admission remain authoritative failures; unknown
emitted scaler warnings instead produce one bounded public review notice naming
the affected heads. Original messages, categories, filenames and line numbers
remain in protected worker evidence, without copying arbitrary text into results.

Known compatibility warnings now capture the H/C scaler head and structured
original/current versions where emitted. Source: the pinned scikit-learn 1.8.0
wheel admitted by requirements/inference.lock, SHA-256
 a0bcfe4d0d14aec44921545fd2af2338c7471de9cb701f1da4c9d85906ab847a,
member sklearn/exceptions.py:160-180 (warning attributes) and
sklearn/base.py:459-469 (serialized _sklearn_version versus runtime __version__).
The latter uses pre-0.18 for a missing serialized version. This inspection was
read-only against the local wheel; no warning-message parsing or new dependency
was introduced. The existing filter and forwarding policy is unchanged.

Public version facts accept bounded release identifiers and that legacy sentinel.
Missing, unsupported or conflicting facts are explicitly unavailable; raw evidence
is retained. Repeated observations collapse into at most one compatibility notice
per head and one additional-warning notice. The full maximum representation is
reserved before inference. Warning heads must occur in the admitted loaded-head
set; a warning for an absent scaler is contradictory protocol evidence, distinct
from an unfamiliar warning about an actually loaded scaler.

Tests were added first: the original policy failed three projection tests; the
absent-head regression independently failed for both H-only and context-only inputs
before correction. Final contained provider tests: 65 in 2.618 seconds; input/
decoder tests: 44 in 0.114 seconds. The near-capacity fixture now includes both
head notices with 32-character versions and the additional-warning notice.
Real worker parity: 2 tests in 43.990 seconds, unchanged predictions, structured
1.5.2 -> 1.8.0 observations. The refreshed public record includes the admitted
warning projection and was extracted only after all executed-source hashes matched.
Focused changed-source Ruff and whitespace checks pass. Three independent reviews
found no residual issue after the absent-head correction. No API publication or
complete AnalysisSession qualification is claimed by this correction.

Direct-coordinate session -- core lifecycle composition
------------------------------------------------------
nmrnet_provider/session.py implements the existing provider_core.analysis session
port for direct JSON only. It delegates preparation to submission.py and result
admission to ResultPlan. Core still owns current server observations, durable
execution entry, terminal retention and publication. The session receives no API
credentials. Composition must supply the allocation-bound worker/host ports and
admitted execution/model/runtime facts; this slice does not implement startup.

Preparation binds the verified input to an active unexecuted acknowledgement and
creates the complete result plan before worker preparation. One session accepts
one preparation, and its exact handle is consumed before execution awaits. Known
InputRejected failures become explicit invalid_input outcomes. Worker/transport
exceptions retain their original identity. If science returns but result/evidence
assembly fails, ResultAssemblyFailed retains the exact received outcome and cause;
evidence overflow never truncates diagnostics or reruns science.

Retirement independently requests local close, awaits host confirmation and observes
local closure. The client retains/shields its original close observation across
timeouts. The session preserves the earliest failure and all later cleanup failures;
cancellation propagates while combined evidence stays attached and retained. A
failed host stop cannot skip local observation, and a local failure cannot skip
host cleanup. No socket-close claim substitutes for host retirement.

Source rationale: provider_core.analysis.AnalysisSession, execution.AttemptExecutor
and publication.TerminalPublisher own the reused lifecycle contracts; their pinned
sibling citations remain in those modules. The integration fixture follows
packages/provider-core/tests/integration/test_execution.py but uses NMRNetSession
rather than the generic recording session. Signed TLS and durable storage are real
within the isolated test; science and host retirement are explicitly recording
ports. Its completion response acknowledges the exact retained scientific result's
schema, size and digest, and the emitted request equals the retained bytes.

Tests first failed because the session module was absent. Regressions cover exact
handle consumption, substituted input, rejected preparation, cancellation, assembly
failure, evidence overflow, retained local close observation, simultaneous cleanup
failures and cancellation with a prior error. Review found the original finally
cleanup ordering could replace an earlier error and skip local observation; the
three explicit cleanup phases correct both issues. Independent failure/mechanical
re-reviews found no residual issue. The initial signed-publication fixture used the
generic result receipt; it was corrected to acknowledge the actual NMRNet bytes.

Real released-model and host-container proof remain separate from this synthetic
science integration. Production allocation/startup, all interpreted/generated/
uploaded routes, recipient rendering, external API/GUI and sibling reuse closure
remain open. Do not mark the package goal complete from this slice.

Final validation for this slice: test/integration/provider-core passes 37 tests in
2.926 seconds; test/unit/provider passes 66 tests in 2.571 seconds. Focused Ruff
and whitespace checks pass. The final failure-order test initially matched the
ordinary word 'private' in safe text; it now checks the actual private messages.
User requested pausing at an opportune boundary; pause after this coherent commit,
before production host composition or another implementation slice.

Crash-durable returned evidence after interrupted acceptance
----------------------------------------------------------
This resolves the earlier P3 in-memory-only outcome limitation for admitted,
bounded AnalysisSuccess/AnalysisFailure values. SuppressedOutcome retains an exact
TerminalPending candidate and a closed reason category: server stop, failed
observation or caller cancellation. The candidate reuses existing request/evidence
admission and capacity; the outer record is never a pending publication. Only the
exact AcknowledgedAttempt -> SuppressedOutcome transition is admitted. There is no
transition back to publication or execution, including extraction of its nested
candidate: the publisher's existing exact-current-record check refuses that too.
Recovery returns the original suppression unchanged without any API/science call.

AttemptExecutor.retain_suppressed_outcome() is an explicit persistence operation,
after the original task and API/storage I/O settle. It writes the already returned
outcome once under the existing claim/CAS owner. It does not change settle()'s
observation-only write contract. A cancelled write remains owned and is observed by
settle(), never redispatched. Successful retention cannot release unconfirmed worker
retirement. Omitted retention continues to block claim release; eligible settlement
diagnostics name the operation and require remaining original work to settle first.
Primary failure remains authoritative when this later persistence/cleanup fails.

Source/rationale: the existing core execution.py observation owner captures results
before acceptance reads, based on the pinned NMRPeak generation-observation source
cited there. Core therefore owns this race and its durable disposition; pushing it
into each binding would duplicate lifecycle authority. TerminalHeld remains reserved
for reviewed API conflict receipts. No scientific policy, exception pickling,
second journal, automatic retry or publication override was introduced.

Tests first failed for the absent record and absent executor method. Normative
witnesses cover exact byte/evidence round trips, pre-entry failures, maximum wire
body/evidence within existing reserved capacity, and close/reopen recovery with
zero sends. Adversarial witnesses cover immutable suppression, malformed reasons,
wrong acknowledgement, final stop, result during failed poll, late result, caller
cancellation, original-write cancellation/settlement, disk failure and unconfirmed
retirement. One retention write and one science call remain the observed counts.
Independent reviews found the eligible action missing from settlement diagnostics,
then found advice that omitted its pending-I/O prerequisite; both were corrected.

Durability scope is precise: candidate scientific bytes/evidence and the suppression
category survive restart. The full original API/acceptance exception chain remains
in memory, not this record. Arbitrary ResultAssemblyFailed.worker_result objects,
unencodable outcomes and unresolved earlier writes are outside this serializer and
remain held for explicit binding/storage reconciliation. This does not close the
remaining recipient diagnostics or full package goal.

Final focused contained checks: normative 35 tests in 0.259 seconds; adversarial
131 in 1.206 seconds; integration 40 in 3.041 seconds (one intentional real-worker
skip). Changed-source Ruff and whitespace checks pass. Repeated independent
failure, mechanical and applicable-U/A reviews found no residual actionable issue
after the diagnostic prerequisite correction. The preceding real-model artifact
continues to identify its exact historical source hashes; it is not recast as a
real-model qualification of this subsequent core change.

Signed Job discovery -- authenticated input identity source
---------------------------------------------------------
ProviderAPI.jobs reads one page from the pinned API's GET /provider/v1/jobs.
job_feed.py records exact route/schema/request/service sources and their rationale.
The caller explicitly supplies analysis kind, provider-attempt presence view, limit
and cursor. The response echoes kind/view, carries at most the requested count and
100 items, and obeys the 65536-byte route ceiling. Immutable items reuse existing
FeedInputFacts; no second input identity/verifier was introduced. Canonical UTC
date parsing now has one shared wire-primitive owner in response_json.py; Attempt
admission uses the same unchanged algorithm.

False presence means selected open Jobs with no prior Attempt by this provider;
true means any prior Attempt state, not active-work inventory. The cursor remains
opaque; only canonical unpadded base64url encoding is checked. Core does not page,
select, schedule, start or retry from discovery. The separate in-progress Attempt
inventory operation and production service composition remain open.

Tests first failed for the absent discovery module. Focused witnesses cover exact
signed/bodyless query construction, explicit view/page choices, schema drift and
kind/view substitution, input identity/type/range checks, invalid calendar/encoding,
cursor canonicalization, requested count, private error evidence and immutable
empty/nonempty pages. A real TLS witness verifies both received signatures and
passes the discovered identity into the existing exact-input read; a second one
cancels discovery and observes retirement of the original transport without retry.
The installed wheel inventory explicitly admits the new core module. No new
dependency, downloader or sibling change was introduced.

Signed in-progress Attempt inventory (after 8630d8a)
==================================================

Purpose: production recovery needs the API's provider-wide live inventory, separate
from Job discovery's any-prior-Attempt view. ProviderAPI.execution_attempts requires
state="in_progress", limit and cursor explicitly, issues one signed bodyless GET,
and returns one immutable fully admitted page. It does not page, claim, resume,
abandon or publish work. An inventory row contains no Job state; a selected
identity can feed the existing read_attempt for an independent fresh observation.
Neither inventory nor that point read supplies local execution history.

Exact authority: I:nmr_api/provider/routes.py:350-365,
provider/execution_attempts_list_request.py, provider/execution_attempts_list_cursor.py,
provider/schemas/execution_attempts_list_response.v1.schema.json and
operations/provider_execution_attempt_list_service.py:38-108. The server restricts
the view to its authenticated provider principal, in-progress Attempts and visible
Jobs across analysis kinds. Cursors are provider/view-bound opaque data. This is
a live traversal, not an atomic snapshot or proof that absent work never existed.
Request/time budgets across pages remain the caller's responsibility.

Implementation keeps schema admission in attempt_inventory.py and reuses signing,
exchange ownership, identity validators and read_timestamp. With two actual page
callers, paging.py now owns their common canonical cursor and 1-100 limit grammar;
there is no generic paginator or policy dispatcher. The start-key grammar has one
neutral require_provider_attempt_key owner in attempt_request.py, used for start
and inventory. Only its operation-specific wording changed. Installed-wheel
inventory and isolated imports include both new modules.

Tests first: normative discovery initially failed with ModuleNotFoundError for
attempt_inventory, before implementation. The new normative/adversarial/TLS tests
exercise exact signed query/no implicit continuation, immutable mixed-kind rows in
both orders, selected identity-to-current-read handoff, complete eager admission,
malformed/private wire refusal, exact route bytes/items, and owner cancellation.
Both existing Job discovery witnesses run unchanged after paging extraction.

Review corrections: fixed the service citation end from 119 to its actual 108.
A proposed capacity concern was measured rather than accepted: 100 rows with
maximum kind/Job/key field lengths serialize to 64,200 bytes with our fixture,
so they fit. Their admission is now tested; the 65,536-byte route ceiling remains
independent of row count and is tested at and one byte above its boundary.
The foreign-retirement test now acts while the original exchange is pending;
completed cleanup correctly leaves no foreign-owned outstanding obligation.
These are local wire/ownership witnesses, not deployed filtering, a production
recovery decision, scientific capacity, full workflow or sibling adoption proof.

Stop observation during execution-entry persistence (after 8d2c9f5)
=================================================================

Problem: AttemptStore already runs filesystem work outside the event loop, but
AttemptExecutor awaited its entry replacement without observing current server
state. A prepared session could therefore remain live until disk completed or the
store's wait expired after cancellation/expiry on the server. The existing fresh
read after successful persistence prevented later execution; it did not provide
prompt retirement during the delay. This slice closes that entry-specific gap.

Authority/rationale: execution.py's pinned NMRPeak execution ordering and generation
observation references remain its behavioral precedent; AttemptStore._observe owns
the existing cancellation-without-cancelling-filesystem contract. The new entry
waiter combines those existing owners rather than introducing a second store or
general supervisor. It polls through the same owner-tagged API and interval,
cancels only the async wait on interruption, then attempts session retirement.
Original filesystem work remains owned. Settlement observes that waiter before
settling original storage; an unresolved waiter blocks both competing storage
settlement and claim release. A completed write cannot override a pending poll's
stop/failure. Cleanup keeps late disk failures secondary to the original cause.
Successful entry still requires the existing fresh state read before execution.

Tests first: holding the real os.replace behind EntryWriteBarrier produced three
expected adverse failures: run did not return/retire before disk release on server
stop, failed poll, and expiry with failed retirement. New tests now require:
- stop/failed observation retires preparation before the blocked disk is released;
  no execute/send, held claim, one original write, explicit late settlement;
- expiry plus unconfirmed retirement and late disk failure retains the primary
  stop and independent protected cleanup/storage evidence, with reuse blocked;
- caller cancellation while both poll and disk are pending retains both owners,
  retires preparation, then settles the originals without another write/request;
- disk completion before a held poll returns stop or failure retains durable entry,
  prevents execution and preserves exact primary/secondary cause ordering;
- an active poll followed by persistence still reaches the fresh state check,
  executes once and retains the terminal outcome before retirement.
The race test observes completion at the public store.replace port before releasing
the poll, rather than inspecting a private task or assuming scheduler order.

Scope deliberately remains execution-entry persistence. Terminal/suppressed-evidence
writes keep their existing behavior. A terminal CAS already submitted can finish
as TerminalPending, which existing settlement/recovery can publish; cancellation
cannot revoke it. TerminalPublisher gates exact durable identity and ownership,
not a fresh Job-state observation. A promise of durable suppression at that later
boundary needs its own explicit disposition/transition design. Terminal-write stop
responsiveness, production startup/restart, all input routes, recipient delivery
and actual sibling qualification therefore remain open under the package goal.

Magnet outcome-seam adaptation fixture (after fca04e3)
==================================================

Peer-source inspection, with one read-only investigator for each sibling, confirmed
the actual adaptation constraints recorded above. It did not authorize sibling
edits. This first contract slice exercises Magnet's outcome distinctions through
the installed core without importing Magnet or executing its scientific code.
It is a proposed post-split adaptation fixture, not qualification of the current
monolithic processor, a production adapter or a sibling migration.

Authority: M:magnet_provider/analysis_contract.py:77-94,145-206;
models/g_01_nmr_forward/provider/magnet_provider_bindings/g_01_nmr_forward/
processor.py:182-283; models/g_01_nmr_forward/magnet_runner_contracts/
runner_g_01_nmr_forward/session.py::encode_model_input; and the scripted rows in
tests/unit/test_g01_provider_processor.py::_response. Exact pin and rationale are
also in tests/fakes/magnet.py. Authored decimal strings, synthetic projection and
noncontiguous atom indices are contract sentinels, not scientific observations,
validated atom-map correspondence, probability normalization or G01 accuracy.
The scripted interpreter supplies already-admitted candidate data; the real
capability's rejection of malformed/unsupported interpretations is not executed
or qualified here. Copying its validator/vocabulary would create another owner.

Three cases in tests/integration/test_magnet_adaptation.py:
- await interpretation of exact verified source bytes before returning a live
  prepared handle; forward unchanged SMILES whitespace and one shared solvent to
  both model heads, then publish exact opaque product bytes, including a future
  binding field and high-precision decimal strings;
- a binding-certified retry-safe interpreter failure before entry remains the
  exact ExecutionFailed.cause with durable NOT_ENTERED. An explicitly selected
  new session may retry that identical admitted request; core does not retry it;
- an indeterminate prediction after entry retains the exact exception and durable
  EXECUTION_ENTERED through store close/reopen. Recovery observes; another executor
  refuses re-entry, so the prediction is never repeated.
The fixture uses g01_local_fixture, not the NMRNet analysis-kind value. This is
local contract input, not a claim that a public API catalog advertises that key.

An initial suggestion that core needed extra availability/indeterminate outcome
types or a readiness port was retracted after tracing the actual owner chain:
binding exceptions plus durable phase already express these distinctions, and
composition can await readiness before starting an Attempt. This fixture adapts
Magnet's returned outcome meanings into binding-owned exceptions; it does not
claim exact source-interface compatibility or implement readiness ordering.

Tests first failed on the absent fixture module in the contract lane. Review moved
the cases to integration: they connect actual runtime owners for a proposed
adaptation rather than verify the current external Magnet interface. Review also
found the first result
expectation reused the fixture's own builder: field loss could change both sides
and go unnoticed. That oracle state was removed from the session. The test now
independently declares the expected envelope/model-input keys and computes its
receipt bytes, then compares decoded content and exact publication bytes. The
shared authored worker payload is source data being preserved, not an independent
scientific prediction oracle; duplicating those sentinel values adds no authority.
No production-core change, new dependency, launcher or scientific implementation.

Open P5 obligations remain: Magnet readiness, cancellation/retirement and classified
publication policy; actual required preparation/prediction split; SECS/NMRPeak
async worker/confirmed-stop fixtures; protocol-change/next-change qualification;
all remaining NMRNet input routes and deployed API/GUI/recipient qualification.
These three local outcome cases close none of those additional obligations.
