P5 scripted child-retirement boundary (2026-10-18)
------------------------------------------------
This slice adds no production owner or supervisor. It exercises the installed
AnalysisSession/AttemptExecutor seam using one fixed authored subprocess inside
make test/integration/provider-core. Source rationale is recorded beside the
fixture: NMRPeak 1697dc84dd1d76ae25b04efc8183fd80614000e5,
nmrpeak_provider/runner_session.py:395-443 closes/retires a channel without claiming
process exit; SECS 34f5915c1534278bbf61b4c6592afa44cf94ab08,
src/secs_inference/provider/worker.py:251-295 reserves stop acknowledgement for the
supervisor that confirms the retained child exited. Merely awaiting a cancelled
Python task or receiving EOF cannot satisfy that ownership contract.

One private peer script and one test session exercise four cases:
1. Scripted preparation completes before a live handle. Generation sees durable
   EXECUTION_ENTERED, runs once, and its expected result reaches the saved terminal
   request. The executor returns only after that exact child exits/is reaped and
   its owned pipes are closed/drained.
2. Scripted preparation rejection selects failure before entry. No generation
   occurs, and the child is still stopped/reaped before the returned failure.
3. A real generation acknowledgement precedes caller cancellation. The exact
   retained child stops, no result/publication candidate is produced, and saved
   execution-entry history survives. Claim release then succeeds.
4. A peer deliberately closes stdout but remains alive, independently of stdin.
   EOF and retirement failure retain the claim; failed settlement does not run
   another generation. Explicit cleanup of that same process still cannot release
   the claim until the original executor settles. No replacement child is started.

The tests first failed on the absent child_session module: 49 discovered cases,
one import error and one explicit live-worker skip. The initial implementation
passed all four new cases. Independent review found an unbounded cancellation
observation could prevent unconditional child cleanup on regression. Cleanup now
uses AsyncExitStack and bounded task observation; child cleanup and store closure
are attempted even if earlier cleanup fails. Executor watchdogs retain their
original task instead of relying on wait_for to finish cancellation indefinitely.
Review also added independent expected-result fields and a claim-release refusal
between physical process cleanup and explicit executor settlement.

The fixture is not a SECS/NMRPeak protocol replica or a scientific validator. Its
preparation acknowledgement establishes order only. It neither qualifies process
startup interruption, descendant processes, resource grants, native inputs,
selection/reselection, real model outcomes, readiness, warm reuse nor production
isolation. Those remain binding/composition obligations. This is additional core
retirement-contract evidence, not closure of either sibling adoption or P5/003.

Source inspection also identified two separate future SECS adoption obligations:
its analysis_run.py:180-218 records entry before worker input_rejected/reselection,
so adoption needs a real prepare/admission split; its existing closed-Job policy
(analysis_run.py:248-265 at the same pin) continues an in-progress Attempt while
the current core executor requires an open
Job. This policy difference needs explicit composition design/tests before claiming
compatibility. SECS outcomes.py:16-51 permits completion only for analysed;
no_starting_candidates remains a failure with the worker's caveat, not a successful
analysis. These source facts are not implemented or qualified by this child test.

P5 explicit Job-state execution policy (2026-10-18)
--------------------------------------------------
Source inspection found a concrete shared-core incompatibility: SECS
34f5915c1534278bbf61b4c6592afa44cf94ab08,
src/secs_inference/provider/analysis_run.py:260-265 deliberately continues an
in-progress Attempt when its Job is closed, but stops for cancellation. The core
executor had required an open Job at every observation, imposing one consumer's
choice on the others. A callback/strategy framework is unnecessary for this finite
state choice. AttemptExecutor now requires an explicit immutable allowed_job_states
frozenset from the existing response owner's vocabulary. All previous callers,
including NMRNet composition tests and the scientific-worker lane, explicitly
choose {open}; the SECS-shaped policy test chooses {open, closed}. There is no
fallback/default. An Attempt that is no longer in_progress always stops, even
when its Job state is allowed. Science and wire ownership remain unchanged.

Tests first failed on the absent paused-session fixture, then on the missing
allowed_job_states keyword (45 normative cases, two failing phase subcases).
After the explicit choice was implemented, normative tests passed. New cases
observe closure during both paused preparation and paused execution, rather than
assuming an initial snapshot proves monitoring behavior. Closed Jobs continue
under the SECS-shaped choice and stop under the previous open-only choice;
cancelled Jobs stop under the SECS-shaped choice. Every terminal Attempt state
crossed with every Job state stops before input/preparation even when closed Jobs
are explicitly permitted. Configuration rejects mutable/empty/unknown/cancelled
choices, including mixed known/unknown sets, before acquiring a claim.

Independent tests-first review found two useful corrections: bound observation
of paused executor tasks so a missing poll cannot hang the suite, and test a
mixed known/unknown policy so nonempty-intersection validation cannot pass. A
shared test-only watchdog retains/cancels/observes the original task. Moving the
known Job-state vocabulary to a frozenset also requires checking raw response
types before membership; malformed list/dictionary observations have explicit
API tests, preserving rejection/evidence instead of leaking a hashability error.

Scope: this corrects the finite Job-state policy only. Failed observations still
stop/retire the core executor. SECS analysis_run.py:252-259 separately tolerates
ApiUnavailable under its existing work deadline; that remains a policy/adoption
obligation. Unit tests use a direct observation port to isolate policy and do not
qualify closed-Job publication or deployed API behavior. No source-only reference
is promoted to such runtime evidence. The already-qualified open-only integration
and real-worker lanes now choose their former behavior explicitly; historical
scientific artifacts are not recertified by this source change. Full sibling
adoption, all input/recipient routes and P4/P5/003 closure remain required.

U02 review rejected an unneeded first-version option to include cancelled in the
permitted Job states. No current consumer needs continued work after cancellation.
Configuration now refuses cancelled alone or in a mixed set; every observation
therefore stops cancelled Jobs. Consumers choose closure behavior only. This
removes an unsupported policy choice instead of adding a new strategy interface.

Next runtime slice: one selected Attempt controller
-------------------------------------------------
The next runtime owner is provider_core.controller.AttemptController: all proposed
behavior is generic lifecycle orchestration over an injected AnalysisSession,
with the already explicit consumer Job-closure policy. NMRNet supplies allocation,
scientific session and host-retirement ports. Its existing live-worker witness
must invoke the new owner; an unused orchestration facade is not acceptance.

Controller ownership transfers only after synchronous configuration validation.
The accepted session then belongs to this single-use owner, including before any
start is sent. The controller retains a private claim from the composition's
shared registry, and the actual current core operation. A read-only observation
of the claim's existing operation owner supports exact settlement; no copied
pending flag or parallel journal/schema is added. Transport/store lifetime stays
with composition. A held controller cannot start another Attempt.

The bounded inventory read is controller-owned. Unresolved retained records
require explicit recovery before fresh work; confirmed records do not globally
block admission. Existing store admission still owns exact duplicate/conflict
and retention-capacity rules. Timed-out/cancelled inventory reads remain owned
until observed; a late read never sends a start. Session cleanup is attempted even
when starting fails or the server returns an already terminal start admission.
That terminal replay returns the original acknowledgement without science or a
new publication choice. No queue, polling service or automatic recovery is added.

The normal path joins existing Starter -> Executor -> Publisher and returns the
existing durable record. Failure preserves the actual operation and primary cause,
plus independent cleanup failures. Explicit settlement observes only original
I/O/work and confirms session retirement before releasing the private claim.
A late acknowledgement cannot enter science; a late TerminalPending cannot trigger
publication. Suppressed-outcome persistence remains an explicit executor operation.
Recovery remains the existing AttemptRecovery owner, after allocation retirement.

Tests first cover success and terminal replay; failed-start cleanup and failed
retirement holding exclusion; cancelled inventory/acknowledgement persistence;
uncertain terminal delivery without rerunning science; unresolved-inventory refusal
and confirmed-inventory admission. Focused installed core lanes precede real
NMRNet/TLS and released-worker/host-retirement composition. This is a finite runtime
step toward a provider; feed scheduling, continuous service, remaining input
routes, recipients and deployed API/GUI qualification still remain open.

R3 source-grounded file-acquisition design checkpoint, 2026-10-22
--------------------------------------------------------------
Design only: no R3 implementation, consumer migration or capacity proof follows.
SECS source inspected at HEAD 7356c354368f2355d3ab5cef00cf48c67cea9c2c;
its worktree was clean. Core uploads.py, transport.py, attempt_store.py and
attempt_files.py were unchanged from HEAD 96a1c48568369af0eab46353a8ee182924be9d6b.
Keep JobUploads.acquire's immutable-byte result. Add an explicit private-file
option through the same membership, grant, budget and HttpTransport exchange
owners; do not buffer the whole Upload in the controller. Composition supplies
the private destination. Charge every consumed chunk before refusal or disk
write, including failed transfers; changing output must not reset any budget.
Permit one bounded outstanding file write, owned across cancelled/expired waits.
Reuse AttemptStore._run/_observe's original-future settlement pattern narrowly;
do not create another downloader, cache, journal or general executor framework.
No successful file receipt before whole-transfer header/length/SHA verification,
file finalization and required network/file I/O settlement. Retain incomplete
private files when writes or closure remain unresolved; settle before unlinking.
Successful-file cleanup belongs to composition after reader quiescence or actual
supervisor-confirmed interrupted-work stop, never merely socket closure.
SECS sources below are relative to src/secs_inference/provider/:
- upload_download.py::download_upload/_copy_verified streams, hashes and closes
  before handing off a path; analysis_run.py::AttemptSources retains source files
  on WorkerStopUnconfirmed; worker.py::WorkerClient.stop requires supervisor proof.
- analysis_run.py::_worker_request currently sends paths only;
  worker_model.py::ScientificHandler.__call__ constructs SourceAccess from them.
- source_access.py::SourceAccess.scope_reader/open/_archive/_preflight_archive
  currently reopens paths; _read_stream already returns immutable ReadSource
  bytes with their digest. input_adapter.py::InputAdapter._verify_components
  retains verified component bytes; input_formats.py::prepare_representation
  consumes them directly or creates controlled scientific temporary files.
Carry authoritative expected Upload length/hash and granted locator to the worker.
Open with O_NOFOLLOW, require a regular file, and verify a bounded immutable byte
snapshot from that opened object before parsing. Path substitution must refuse
or yield the same verified bytes; inode identity grants no scientific authority.
Adapt ZIP sniff/preflight/ZipFile to BytesIO over that snapshot; never reopen the
original path after verification. Hash -> seek -> parse on a mutable descriptor
is unsafe against in-place mutation. Existing worker byte materialization makes
this a local adaptation; sealing, memfd paths and new IPC are not justified here.
Worker memory is not constant: compressed snapshots and expanded member bytes
remain bounded but coexist. Actual worker capacity still needs qualification.
Focused tests first: shared failed-byte budgets; streamed file success; digest,
length/header/write/finalization refusal; cancelled pending write before cleanup;
primary plus cleanup failure; pathname/symlink/in-place substitution before or
during snapshot; immutable consumption afterward; ZIP limits and stop-held files.

Awaited condition reporting qualified, 2026-10-22
================================================
Commit 3b21d8f6599f5818b76172d2312196f950a1d90d implements the R4 condition
port and supersedes the earlier design-only status. This is a narrow shared
lifecycle capability; observation-gap policy, peer admission/refusal, warm reuse,
full R4, P4/P5 and 003 remain open.

AnalysisSession.prepare and execute now require the keyword-only report_condition
callable. The binding calls it with a reviewed public condition code, or None to
clear it, and awaits the acknowledgement. It receives no raw API client or phase
selector. Core binds the Attempt and current phase. It serializes reporting with
observation/progress API operations and admits the acknowledgement before the
await returns. Running-progress acknowledgement, durable execution entry and
fresh observation still precede science.
The callable expires when its session phase returns or core stops observing it;
a report waiting behind an API operation rechecks that lifetime before sending.
Calls are sequential and awaited; detached/concurrent reporting is unsupported.

A failed or cancelled report cannot be ignored to authorize the next phase or
publication, even if the binding catches it. Existing first-owned failures and
independent causes remain preserved; this does not give a report precedence over
an earlier observation or server-stop failure. A later binding failure survives
as context without replacing the report's original transport/cancellation cause.
A returned outcome can still exist in memory without a durable terminal record
or confirmed API effect. The actual operator stderr and failure.json now say
outcome_in_memory/unconfirmed_record_in_memory; they make no durable-retention
claim. Receiving tests preserve useful primary, later-context and independent
cleanup causes while excluding credential canaries and scientific payloads.
Those tests do not qualify commissioner GUI or requester API error delivery.

Raw streamed HTTPS file output qualified, 2026-10-22
===================================================
Commit: 3cd984387cdde1889d16c24f022241cf91592a71. This closes the transport
portion of R3, not verified-file acquisition or consumer qualification. Exact
implementation: packages/provider-core/src/provider_core/transport.py,
HttpTransport.exchange_file/_exchange/_perform and private _FileBody. The byte
exchange API stays intact. No dependency, grant, retry or scientific policy changed.

exchange_file borrows an exclusively used writable regular-file descriptor
without append mode, at the caller's current position. Its HttpFileResponse
contains status/headers and received body count/SHA-256, without a buffered body.
Those are raw response observations, not whole-file, future-path, verified Upload
or durability facts. Failed cleanup can leave even all response bytes written
without permitting handoff. The caller owns descriptor closure and path cleanup
and must preserve them until transport retirement is confirmed.

One concrete per-exchange executor validates the descriptor before any network
owner is created, then writes at most 65536 bytes per submitted operation. It
handles short writes; no progress refuses. Each received chunk is charged before
limit refusal or writing. There is one outstanding original future, no queued write backlog;
the next network chunk cannot be consumed until the write settles. On interruption,
network cleanup precedes original-future settlement; a blocked inspection/write
keeps the existing transport owner held. Late disk errors and independent network
cleanup errors remain protected evidence alongside cancellation/deadline causes.

Rationale/provenance: the preceding R3 checkpoint cites the inspected SECS stream
and source-consumption owners at 7356c354368f2355d3ab5cef00cf48c67cea9c2c.
Original-future settlement adapts this package's attempt_store.py::_run/_observe
at parent 36eddeb485697a845ff445e5e0fbd950a52177ef; the journal itself is not reused
as an acquisition owner. Borrowing the already-open descriptor keeps file creation,
grant admission and reader-safe cleanup with their actual owners. Separate raw
file facts avoid pretending an empty HttpResponse.body contains transferred bytes.

Tests-first: initial absent-port RED ran 22 tests (1 failure/6 errors); descriptor
preflight RED then ran 29 tests (5 failing descriptor subcases). A fresh independent
audit identified that valid-integer closed/read-only descriptors could otherwise
cause network work first. The repair checks regular/writable/nonappend state in
the same original-future owner. Explicit EBADF and read-only/append/pipe fixtures
prove zero backend/client creation. Held fstat tests cover cancellation and expiry,
then successful original inspection: no late request starts. Held real write
tests cover repeated cancellation, one write/no read-ahead, late ENOSPC with clean
or failed network cleanup, owner exclusion and protected cause rendering.

Verified Upload file acquisition qualified, 2026-10-22
=====================================================
Commit b4b73e70d4e5e9f0733dcabdbd875393a0940218 adds
uploads.py::JobUploads.acquire_file/settle_file and VerifiedUploadFile. This closes
R3's acquisition owner, not worker consumption or the user's remaining003 gates.
The byte and file paths share selection, fresh membership/grant, exact store
request, digest/length/header verification and cumulative byte/request/deadline
accounting. No second downloader, cache, automatic retry or budget reset exists.

Composition lends an open, exclusively controlled effective-user-owned0700
directory. Before grant issuance, acquisition creates one exclusive0600 leaf;
restrictive umask does not change its intended mode. Success follows verified
transfer, confirmed close and a final source-deadline check. The receipt binds
Job/Upload/length/hash to that transfer, not future pathname contents or durability.
Successful ownership leaves the acquisition owner; composition must wait for
reader quiescence before deleting the source. Worker snapshot verification remains
required before parsing; no consumer migration is claimed.

One original create/close/remove future is retained through cancellation/expiry.
Cleanup uses its explicit grace without granting more source-work budget. A late
successful operation can finish abort cleanup only, never issue a late grant or
receipt. Pending transport writes must retire before descriptor closure/unlink.
A close error is not retried by descriptor number, even after descriptor reuse.
Unknown original inode identity and observed replacement have distinct refusals;
neither permits guessed deletion. Trusted directory-owner inspection/removal can
allow later confirmed-absence settlement. This relies on exclusive directory
control, not atomic conditional unlink against concurrent same-user mutation.

Immutable Upload file consumption boundary qualified, 2026-10-22
===============================================================
Commit4848a4f2bfb42e11d7a8e9e38b1f8c93efb92062 adds
uploads.py::read_upload_file/_read_upload_snapshot and UploadFileReadFailed.
It accepts VerifiedUploadFile, an explicit borrowed directory descriptor and an
explicit worker byte ceiling, returning the existing VerifiedUpload byte result.
It opens one non-dot basename with O_NOFOLLOW/O_NONBLOCK/O_CLOEXEC, requires a
regular file, reads chunks <=65536 bytes and no more than expected length+1, then
uses VerifiedUpload's existing length/hash verifier once. Observed close precedes
return. It neither parses nor unlinks the source. Changed paths after open cannot
redirect consumption; mutation during copying refuses unless the returned bytes
still exactly match the transfer receipt. Later changes cannot alter immutable
data. This is not an atomic filesystem snapshot or constant-memory/durability proof.

The synchronous function belongs inside an already supervised reader. Its unit
interruption witness injects cancellation from the read operation; it does not
prove an awaiting controller's cancellation reaches synchronous I/O or that a
worker stopped. Worker supervision and source lifetime remain binding obligations.
No new executor, IPC variant, archive policy or grant authority was introduced.
Existing NMRNet readers already receive immutable bytes; they were not migrated.

Precise source/rationale: the preceding R3 checkpoint inspects SECS at
7356c354368f2355d3ab5cef00cf48c67cea9c2c. Paths relative to
src/secs_inference/provider: source_access.py::_read_stream establishes immutable
ReadSource contents; input_adapter.py::_verify_components and
input_formats.py::prepare_representation consume retained bytes. Current SECS
path reopenings in scope_reader/open/_archive/_preflight_archive and its path-only
worker request are the concrete future adaptation seam. This implementation copies
no archive/scientific policy into core and edits no sibling. Basename admission
protects dirfd authority, not inferred molecular identity or a new file-mode rule.

A separate read failure is necessary: acquisition owns grant issuance; this
reader cannot report grant_issued=false for an already acquired file. Exact-owner
process_reports.py admission now renders UploadFileReadFailed's safe read reason,
protected cause, independent cleanup and interruption, without acquisition facts.
The pure byte verifier maps its constructor-owned safe reason at the read boundary;
its translated refusal is raised outside that handler so no synthetic acquisition
context survives. Global context traversal is unchanged. Actual filesystem causes
remain private under .cause, suppressed from ordinary tracebacks; no generic
exception stringify was added. A close error is never retried by fd number and
refuses handoff. Operation and cleanup remain independently visible. Cancellation
raised within the worker stays the original object, retaining previous cause and
close failure through the existing owned-link pattern. Unconfirmed closure requires
supervisor retirement before source release; the primitive grants no stop proof.

Both functions expose PROTECTED, UNREDACTED data, never a public report:
- owned_details returns fresh scalar facts for exact reviewed core types. Unknown
  types/subclasses acquire no text or attribute access. No raw response body,
  unrelated headers, terminal bytes or scientific payload is serialized.
- owned_relationships borrows original protected cause/cleanup/interruption
  references, preserving labels, order, repeated references and absent causes.
  Standard __cause__/__context__/groups, cycles, node/display limits and redaction
  remain with the caller's existing mixed graph.
- Received status/request IDs use the existing correlation owner, including its
  single visible-ASCII header/128-byte admission. Chat keeps complete-response
  precedence over a transport head and reads late received metadata at projection
  time. There is no copied/stale header authority.
- Grant issuance is an acquisition fact, absent on reader failures. In-memory
  outcome/record presence, received start acknowledgement, held refusal and
  matching terminal receipt remain separate facts. No flag grants resend,
  scientific repetition or durable-acceptance authority.
NMRNet's existing walker still redacts every selected string before bounding it,
walks the one mixed graph and applies its scientific summaries and actual sinks.
No previously unreviewed core exception vocabulary is added by this extraction.

2026-10-22 R5b: explicit read-only recovery, qualified at2343e63.
TerminalRefusalPolicy.READ_STATE is an opt-in consumer choice on controller,
publisher and pending-command recovery. After a terminal HTTP404/409 that does
not establish an operation-valid TerminalHeld, the publisher durably records
TerminalReadRequired before returning; it performs no GET. Verified404 already
has resource_not_found, so 'unclassified' here means no valid operation hold,
not problem_code=None. Reviewed conflicts retain TerminalHeld. The existing
RETAIN_PENDING default and other statuses/transport failures without404/409 heads
are unchanged; NMRNet has not silently acquired the SECS no-resend policy.

Explicit AttemptRecovery on the read-required record performs one GET, then
retains an identity-bound state observation. Only the existing API owner's fully
admitted404/resource_not_found Problem permits TerminalReadNotVisible; bare404,
malformed replies and mismatched identities preserve the read-only obligation.
Reopening already-observed/not-visible states performs no request. Nested pending
commands cannot publish. Even observed succeeded still blocks fresh admission;
no GET result confirms the exact original terminal bytes or authorizes science.
The precedent/source rationale and exact SECS links are in R5a above and the new
record docstring. No second URL/Problem parser, journal or scheduler was added.

Observed-but-not-durable records keep their claim and original write until
settlement. Failed writes and unexpected storage defects retain the original
cause plus observed record. Settlement observes original API/storage work only;
it neither repeats GET/POST/write nor appends synthetic failures on repeat polls.
The publisher retains the original failed send in last_failure even when returning
its durable read-only disposition. A received404/409 head with held transport
cleanup raises that protected failure before controller claim release; later
cleanup failure preserves its disposition/primary/API evidence. This is in-memory
protected evidence, not a durable diagnostic history or discharge of R6.

2026-10-22 current retention/capacity contract and operator guidance.
The supported policy is keep-all within maximum_records: confirmed completions
and failures retain their requests, receipts and protected evidence and still
occupy slots. AttemptFiles._require_new_record_capacity refuses fresh admission
at the limit; no expiry, automatic reclamation or record deletion is implemented.
This is a finite operating limit, not an unfinished requirement for a new archive
or diagnostic journal. Broader diagnostic-history behavior remains separate.

The full-store StoreRefused now explains that finished Attempts count and tells
the operator to preserve records, stop/settle, then increase maximum_records with
enough disk space before restarting. docs/provider.md's full-store procedure
specifies retained scope/files, stopped inspection, a fresh INSTALLATION_ROOT,
and normal startup's unresolved-obligation/API/disk checks. Raising the slot limit
neither resolves retained work nor reserves physical space. The recovery guide
also explains read-required versus already-observed/not-visible records: no
terminal resend, no matching receipt inferred from a read, unresolved remains so.

2026-10-22 safe-read outage disposition at the existing API owner.
ProviderAPI.can_retry_read consumes only this API's current ApiFailure, a GET
method supplied by its signed-exchange owner, and confirmed original retirement.
It re-reads late causes/headers rather than caching an earlier decision. Reviewed
timeouts/native connection errors and temporary DNS failure qualify; certificate,
unknown/protocol/local permission failures, failed cleanup and all mutations do
not. The native/httpcore cause walk is bounded; unknown wrappers refuse.

502/503/504 may permit another safe read without claiming transience or parsing
the error body as API authority. This deliberately includes HTML maintenance
responses and unknown/malformed error Problems, including an edge's expected
topology marker. Wrong/duplicate explicit topology still refuses. Requiring a
verified JSON Problem here would turn an ordinary proxy outage into a manual
restart. Read-retry permission grants no receipt, mutation replay, scientific
dispatch or automatic exchange. Service pacing/diagnostics are the next slice.

2026-10-22 U27 first service slice: recover admission/discovery reads.
The service now uses API090f6ea's settled GET disposition in its original startup
or work task. A failed page abandons the complete admission/discovery cycle:
retry rechecks local capacity and live inventory before walking discovery from
cursor=None. It never wraps allocation, controller.run, mutations or science.
Original API retirement precedes the retry decision; failed retirement preserves
the original read and cleanup failure and dispatches nothing further.

ServicePolicy/config require read_backoff_max_seconds>=idle_seconds. The nominal
delay starts at idle_seconds, doubles to that cap, and each actual wait draws
uniformly from half to all of the nominal delay. Only a completed whole read cycle
resets it. Requests retain their configured deadlines; the outage itself can wait
until recovery or stop. The existing stop event interrupts backoff. Startup I/O
already in flight remains subject to its exchange/retirement bounds.

Process-supplied callbacks project the original failure through failure_evidence
before emitting api_read_retry/api_read_recovered notices. One nonblocking stderr
write restores the original descriptor flags before yielding. FIFO notices cannot
exceed PC_PIPE_BUF: refusal happens before any bytes, preserving the next JSON
record. DiagnosticOutputRefused exposes only code-owned numeric/reason text.
The latest failed notice's event, outage and output cause remain bounded in memory
and reach private result.json as api_notice_failure on released shutdown. A notice
sink failure alone does not stop service or alter science's outcome. If a separate
operation fails, its report includes that same notice evidence.

Independent review found two necessary output corrections: direct stderr.write
could block on pipe backpressure, including the final failure-report path, and a
large nonblocking notice could leave partial JSON. The existing _failure_report
now shares nonblocking stderr output while still attempting independent file
publication; no new logging task, store, journal or lifecycle owner was added.
Captured text-sink tests explicitly adapt the owned byte-writer; their diagnostic,
redaction and held-ownership assertions are unchanged. The held subprocess's old
stderr.write fault injection also moved to that actual writer after a rerun showed
it no longer intercepted the effect. No assertion was weakened.

2026-10-22 U27 Hello service recovery.
Initial/periodic Hello now use037fd97's exact-operation permission after original
transport retirement. The service's one private pacing owner serves two fixed
callers: complete admission/discovery GET cycles, and newly constructed current
Hello snapshots. There is no retained-body replay, general mutation retry policy,
new scheduler or journal. Each send calls the existing snapshot supplier again,
so an outage cannot force an old offering to be republished. The first accepted
Hello still gates on_started/discovery/allocation; a periodic outage leaves the
already owned scientific task running. Permanent protocol/authentication errors,
failed retirement and active Attempt failures retain their existing stop policy.

The required cap is now service.api_backoff_max_seconds, shared by both operation
kinds with independently reset outage waits; no old-key alias was added. All
maintained config/test callers were changed. Each request still has finite
exchange/retirement limits, backoff is stop-aware, and outage duration itself has
no retry-count ceiling. This change is source/local qualification; the previously
captured controller image does not contain it.

Unconfirmed Hello evidence is captured at _publish_current itself, including
initial startup before a Hello task exists. A later failed/cancelled send replaces
earlier outage evidence; only a receipt for the latest sent snapshot clears the
slot. The old periodic-cancellation copying branch was removed. Protected shutdown
result.json now calls this unconfirmed_hello, covering backoff and interruption;
failure.json includes it if another operation/reporting sink fails. api_hello_retry
and api_hello_recovered use the existing bounded, redacted, nonblocking notice
sink. Review corrected current_announcement_confirmed to latest_hello_send_confirmed:
the receipt confirms the sent snapshot, without attesting to later supplier or
directory state. Its preceding-failure evidence remains explicitly separate.

2026-10-22 U27 bounded active-observation owner, before production wiring.
Core now accepts an optional ObservationRecovery(gap_seconds,backoff_max_seconds,
on_retry,on_recovered), passed unchanged by Controller to Executor. None retains
core's fail-stop contract. The aggregate groups the one finite recovery behavior
and its required protected observers; it adds no supervisor, journal, scheduler,
dependency or alternate lifecycle. Production NMRNet must explicitly supply it
from required configuration and its existing notice sink in the next slice.

The original executor owns the complete read window and its retained observation
task. Its existing API lock stays held through failed-read retirement, reviewed
GET permission, capped jittered pacing and fresh read; queued progress cannot
overtake unresolved I/O. New phases, execution entry, accepting a handle/outcome
and terminal choice still require a fresh allowed identity-bound observation.
Only that GET can repeat. START/progress/terminal mutation and science behavior
are unchanged. Unexpected observer errors retain the original API cause through
the existing ExecutionFailed field; ordinary sink failure handling stays with the
process callback owner, not a new core catch-all.

Initial timeout-context draft was rejected in review: cancelling the awaited HTTP
read can wait through its separate transport-retirement grace and delay science's
stop. The final code uses asyncio.wait on the retained original observation task,
cancels it once at expiry, and retires science before bounded joining of read
cleanup. A pending waiter blocks claim release, suppressed-outcome retention and
parallel API retirement. Settlement never sends another GET or resumes science.
Cancellation with an independent read-retirement cause remains protected cleanup
evidence. Expiry is ObservationGapExpired, not a bad-input result or permission to
fail/restart an Attempt. Existing OBSERVATION_FAILED suppression can preserve a
completed result without publication after original tasks/I/O settle.

Review also rejected borrowing executor-lifetime last API failure: after recovery,
a later lock-wait expiry could blame an earlier503. Current-window failure/read-
started facts now belong to the actual observation; its expired exception borrows
that owner for late-arriving read evidence. A lock-wait expiry invents no read or
read cause, while independently interrupted progress keeps its own PUT evidence.
Monotonic checks before GET, after response and parent acceptance refuse late
authority even when event-loop timeout callback delivery is delayed. Error text
says after starting a read, covering subsequent retirement/backoff as well.

Established sources, consulted2026-10-22 at the user's request:
  Python3.12 asyncio Task documentation, Timeouts and Waiting Primitives:
    https://docs.python.org/3.12/library/asyncio-task.html#timeouts
    https://docs.python.org/3.12/library/asyncio-task.html#waiting-primitives
  The timeout primitive requests cancellation; wait returns pending tasks without
  cancelling them. We adapt these primitives to existing executor/task ownership,
  rather than treating elapsed wait or requested cancellation as native retirement.
  Marc Brooker, Timeouts, retries, and backoff with jitter, AWS Builders' Library,
  published2019-12-05; Retries and backoff / Jitter sections:
    https://aws.amazon.com/builders-library/timeouts-retries-and-backoff-with-jitter/
  (redirects to https://builder.aws.com/content/3EumjoZascWd1oZiEgL8ORlv3qE/timeouts-retries-and-backoff-with-jitter)
  Adaptation: one operation-owned retry layer, capped jitter and explicit deadlines;
  timeout is not evidence that side effects did not occur. Mutations therefore
  retain their exact owner-specific replay/reconciliation contracts.

2026-10-22 U27 controller-owned exact terminal recovery, before product wiring.
AttemptController accepts explicit TerminalRecovery(window_seconds,
backoff_max_seconds,on_retry,on_recovered); None preserves core fail-stop behavior.
The existing controller repeats only the same TerminalPublisher.publish with the
same immutable TerminalPending. Scientific retirement was already confirmed by
the executor before this seam. The publisher retains durable-before-send equality
checks, exact body bytes, fresh signing and receipt-before-confirmation storage.
No controller.run, START, interpreter, geometry or prediction is restarted.

Only an actual PublicationFailed whose primary cause is its API send failure,
with no received outcome or independent settlement failure, enters consideration.
The original publisher settles its own claim-borrowed I/O first; API eligibility
is then re-read, including late evidence. A receipt-storage error cannot borrow a
previous send's availability classification. Failed retirement preserves the send
as primary and its real cleanup failure independently, even if the retry window
has expired. Explicit refusals/holds and malformed received receipts remain under
the existing publisher dispositions; they do not enter recovery.

Window meaning is deliberate: from the first candidate send failure, include
retirement, bounded synchronous observers and capped jittered backoff; begin no
new publisher invocation after expiry. An already admitted invocation may finish
its existing bounded storage/HTTPS/retirement later. No second timeout task or
lifetime registry was added. The post-observer deadline is checked again before
sleep and retry, so an observer cannot add another full wait after expiry.
TerminalRecoveryExpired exposes the last PublicationFailed through the existing
exact-owner protected projection and keeps the pending command unchanged.
Callbacks borrow the actual acknowledgement and protected API failure; successful
recovery identifies the original outage while each retry reports its actual send.

Next U27 resource slice: confirmed-history ownership contract (planned, not implemented)
====================================================================================
Current source checkpoint:d97bfaf. Owners: provider_core/attempt_files.py::
AttemptFiles._inventory/admit/_publish/_require_capacity; attempt_store.py::
AttemptStore._run/settle; nmrnet_provider/service.py::_work_loop/_admission.
Current maximum_records counts finished records forever. Separately,
tools/provider_workers.sh::provider_workers_service counts retired request leaves
forever. Neither counter is a sustained-Job retention policy; increasing limits or
fresh-root rotation is not the proposed repair.

Preservation/envelope decision before coding:
- Keep every confirmed record's ORIGINAL bytes, including private scientific and
  diagnostic evidence, inside the existing private scoped storage grant. A terminal
  receipt is not permission to discard evidence. No TTL, deletion, offload endpoint,
  external upload, new retention journal/index or secret-bearing metadata is added.
- Reuse active slots by explicitly archiving only exact TerminalConfirmed records.
  Other terminal/held/suppressed/read-required/unresolved records stay active.
  Archival itself releases no lifecycle claim and proves no native retirement.
  Composition may request it only after its original controller has released work,
  or while reconciling an already clean scoped startup inventory; normal remote
  admission gates still run before fresh allocation. records/admission_inventory
  remain read-only; no hidden archive mutation belongs in those APIs.
- Logical active slots and historical diagnostics are different resources. All
  history is retained, so physical usage grows. Existing actual filesystem
  headroom/filesystem_reserve_bytes remains the envelope: stop safely before its
  floor if evidence cannot be retained. A free-space check is not a reservation
  against other writers. No indefinite throughput, automatic historical disposal,
  dedicated-volume quota or bounded total history size is claimed. Retention/offload
  beyond that requires a real policy/destination rather than invented authority.

Store mechanism to implement/tests first:
- Add explicit archive_confirmed(expected,owner=...) through the SAME original-future
  worker/settlement gate. Timeout/cancellation retains that operation, not a replay.
- Keep current active filenames/codecs. One private history-<scope digest> directory
  uses canonical existing RecoveryScope facts, mode0700/same UID/same filesystem,
  nofollow and the root's existing flock. Its exact name keeps an otherwise empty
  active root bound to its original scope. Foreign history names hold startup.
  This is local scope metadata, not authentication of unscanned history.
- Reuse original-file admission to obtain decoded record AND the raw bytes actually
  read. Copy those bytes, not encode_record(expected). Reuse the existing durable
  file publisher with an explicit destination directory; history staging has a
  deterministic record-key name so active/reused-key point checks can detect an
  interrupted copy without scanning all historical files on each Job.
- Sync the copied file, confirm descriptor close and sync history BEFORE retiring
  the active entry; then sync the active directory. Preserve stage/copies on any
  uncertain effect. Identical completed duplicates can be finished by another
  explicit archive operation; partial/conflicting/invalid counterparts hold.
- Before new admission, check the archived provider-attempt-key filename and refuse
  reuse, including identical old start facts. Archived confirmation is not an
  executable replay obligation. Root scope and key checks survive restart even
  when active inventory is empty. Current active inventory remains bounded;
  arbitrary history corruption is not silently advertised as fully audited.
- Test exact noncanonical raw-byte/max-evidence preservation, more Jobs than active
  slots, archived-key reuse refusal, changed-scope and permission/symlink refusal,
  copy/sync/unlink uncertainty, identical duplicate restart, and held original
  archive I/O. Keep useful phase diagnosis through StoreFailed and the existing
  protected receiving projection; preserve both primary and independent close.
- Document actual historical file locations/lookup and the active-only recovery
  view; preserve portable standalone records. Do not add a new operator command
  or general history-query framework merely for this slice.

Native request renewal is a separate following owner change. Only AFTER the
controller has consumed exact host retirement and settled original setup I/O may
it acknowledge that token as consumed. Host must independently know its exact
worker is retired before moving that token's retained request/receipt/diagnostic
files into private history and releasing active maps/slots. Publishing a retired
receipt alone is too early: the original client may still need it. Archive rather
than erase evidence, refuse old-token reuse, retain unresolved native leaves, and
reuse the same physical disk envelope. No second lifetime registry/recycler or
scientific rerun is planned. These are designs, not passed qualification claims.

Pre-implementation mechanism refinement, after independent design challenge:
Use an exclusive DIRECT final history leaf, not generalized _publish/history
staging. Share only the existing bounded write-all/file-fsync loop. A partial
copy is then found by the same active-key point check and holds storage while the
complete original remains active. A complete identical active/history duplicate
can be resynced and finished by explicit archival. There is no extra staging
namespace or stage/target hard-link pair to reconcile.

Same-filesystem linking was considered. Current original-file admission requires
st_nlink==1 to reject aliases outside the private namespace. A crash after linking
active into history and before unlinking active creates nlink2, so the shortcut
would require a second paired-path/inode admission rule or block reopening a valid
interrupted archive. Retain the singly-linked invariant and copy the bounded raw
record instead. This also avoids a staging-link intermediate state inside history.
One full temporary body copy is already covered by _require_capacity(inventory,
additional_records=0); staging/final directory names would alias, not consume two
body copies. Never weaken ordinary hard-link admission for this optimization.
This refinement supersedes the planned deterministic staging/generalized-publisher
bullets above. Source: current AttemptFiles._read/_publish and StoreLimits.

storage. Complete identical duplicates can finish explicitly after reopening.
History-only roots retain scope binding and old keys cannot become new Attempts.
records/admission_inventory remain read-only active views, not full history audits.

complete duplicate incorrectly required another copy's free-space headroom. Fixed

control refuses before creating history. Reviewed record_visible documentation
now means an observed target entry, which can be partial or pre-existing; it

retain useful copy/removal/sync phase messages plus original/independent causes.

Next native renewal slice: host request-consumption protocol (plan before coding)
==============================================================================
Owners: tools/provider_workers.sh::provider_workers_service/retire/publish and
nmrnet_provider/allocation.py::WorkerAllocation._retire; retirement.py::
retire_worker_channel. Exact retirement currently leaves requests and host maps
counted forever. A consumed TOKEN request is a separate client acknowledgement,
not a second retirement or permission to rerun science. The client may publish it
only after original setup I/O, exact host retirement and channel closure settle.
Host-only support is the first bounded slice; client handoff follows separately.

Keep receipts/TOKEN at its current stable read-only grant, including result and
resources.json. Move owned/TOKEN then requests/TOKEN into private history/TOKEN,
using same-filesystem directory renames after their writers stop. This preserves
all original diagnostic/request bytes and the resources.json hardlink without a
recursive copy/delete, new controller grant or second lifetime registry. Confirmed
never-dispatched tokens have no owned tree; host's existing retired state plus
empty ID/role proves that case. Absence of a tree alone proves nothing.

Only exact consumed bytes and independently known retired state permit this move.
After both namespace transfers succeed, publish archived TOKEN at the existing
stable receipt path, then release the existing maps before servicing another
request. Process eligible consumed leaves before counting active capacity. Old
receipt/history tokens are refused before any new create/map initialization.
Any partial move/publication error preserves remaining paths and held maps;
subsequent service must refuse unresolved transfers, not recreate or silently
discount them. A missing/mismatched resource-report receipt for an otherwise
admitted final report prevents renewal, preserving the earlier retention error.

Durability scope: the current Bash host request/receipt protocol confirms observed
filesystem effects; it has no directory-fsync primitive. These renames preserve
observed namespace/content during the installation, not power-loss durability.
Do not claim durable native history or substitute broad syncfs. The core Attempt
record remains separately fsynced/durable. Restart still refuses reuse of a prior
native installation root and requires explicit stopped reconciliation. This
bounded host capability does not yet enable automatic client consumption.

Native history can reside on a different filesystem from store_root. Its retained
disk usage has no independently configured native free-space reserve in the
current Bash owner. Do not imply AttemptStore's fstatvfs/reserve covers that mount.
An actual native write/rename/receipt error holds renewal with every remaining
path and existing map retained; no quota, offload or implicit cleanup is invented.

Tests first in existing test/unit/provider/allocation: more completed role tokens
than the active request bound; exact retained request/diagnostic/report bytes and
private modes; retired-but-unconsumed retention; malformed/premature consumption;
old-token reuse; partial rename/marker publication and resource retention failures
hold accounting without engine re-execution. No paid/native science rerun needed.

Next client slice: explicit adopted-channel retirement
====================================================
Add retire_channel(worker) to NMRNet's retirement port: fixed HostRetirement
reuses the existing close/retire/wait_closed sequence; WorkerAllocation adds

framework is needed. The private consumption method additionally requires the
original allocation/setup and exact retirement tasks to have completed cleanly.
One shielded publication/observation task publishes consumed once; completed
absence/deadline observations can later read again, while publication, malformed
receipt or descriptor-cleanup failures remain held. Keep the completed staging
link because the host may rename the whole request tree immediately after the
exclusive consumed link becomes visible; no post-visibility unlink is safe.

unadopted-writer cleanup keep their existing complete failure set/settlement path
and leave host request/receipt evidence unconsumed for stopped inspection. Those
operational setup failures already end service; they are not successful throughput
whose slots need renewal. Do not broaden consumption from _retire's local empty
failure list when outer close/setup failures remain. Correctable source input
rejections still use adopted channels and can consume after their clean retirement.

OOM strategy discussion and applicability
========================================

01a1644e-f84a-7ad1-9749-198734febb41, then continued the

Rationale retained from discussion: a fresh READY or released flock alone is not
proof that an unreaped old worker is gone. Replacement requires the actual
configured composition's container/PID-namespace retirement fence. A future
model-specific pure-compute retry must keep frozen input/Attempt/seed/decoding,
bounded executions and original/second protected causes, distinguish definite
failure from lost response, and leave cancellation/protocol/rejection/cleanup
failure out of that retry. Fixed seed is not a GPU bitwise-reproducibility claim.
Never resubmit a Job, rerun interpretation, duplicate terminal mutations or raise
resource limits merely to hide OOM. NMRNet adds no automatic OOM/inference retry
in this work; its current native-failure ownership and retained evidence remain.

Controller START slice qualified
--------------------------------
StartRecovery admits bounded retries through the SAME AttemptStarter.resume and

starts when the first send failure is observed; it includes retirement, observers
and capped jittered backoff. No new invocation starts after expiry; admitted I/O

after the retry window. Adversarial proof: auth/conflict/malformed success/topology/

Condition-report recovery: settled design before implementation
---------------------------------------------------------------
AnalysisSession (provider_core/analysis.py:65-74) already requires sequential,
awaited reporting and excludes detached/concurrent calls. The pinned progress
PUT's latest_progress recovery permits the same still-current phase/code with
fresh signing. It does not require a GET per resend, and a GET cannot inspect
condition currentness. Keep _monitor the sole lifecycle-observation owner.

Use separate opt-in ConditionRecovery(window_seconds, backoff_max_seconds,
on_retry, on_recovered), default None fail-stop. API.can_retry_condition supplies
settled exact current PUT/Attempt evidence; the reporter owns current facts.
Inline recovery retains one pending awaited condition. Settle/classify under the
existing API lock, release it during bounded paced waiting so the monitor can
observe stop, then recheck phase activity, failure latch and retry-admission
deadline under that lock before resending. Return only after matching progress
receipt; no silent deferral, scientific/model retry, observation waiter, condition
queue or generation history. An overlap refusal can poison the existing latch
before an old condition is resent if a binding violates the sequential contract.

Retry admission begins at first failed send and includes retirement, notices,
lock wait and jittered backoff. Existing bounded I/O admitted before expiry keeps
its owner; a timer creates no replacement task or proof of stopped work. Preserve
first/current fault and independent cleanup evidence. Expiry stays operational and
must join the existing suppressed-outcome rules if a binding catches it and later
returns science. Protected notices identify actual Attempt/phase/condition and
say recovered only after matching acknowledgement. Conditions remain code-owned
public vocabulary; never derive them from exception/model/input text.

Tests first: actual current/cleared-condition retry after settled transient fault;
monitor read during backoff, observed server stop preventing resend; matching ACK
before exactly one dependent effect; cancellation/cleanup/late fault/foreign or
permanent refusal; deadline while waiting for the lock; overlap and swallowed
failure poisoning. Reuse condition and executor fixtures and focused lanes.

Bounded prediction recovery connected
---------------------------------------
NMRNetSession optionally owns PredictionRecovery with an allocation callback and
process-owned observer; production supplies it explicitly. Only its execute call
catches admitted PredictionExecutionFailed, owned WorkerExchangeTimedOut or
EOF/reset/broken pipe. Preparation/refusal/protocol/caller cancellation and
ResultPlan.finish stay outside eligibility. At most two execute calls, using the
same frozen request tuple and a new native prepare/token. The original ResultPlan
still checks the complete returned batch/model bytes/runtime; no partial merge.

Before replacement, the original retire_channel must confirm exact host removal,
local close and allocation consumption/archive. Factory.allocate_prediction
reuses only the existing fixed prediction profile; it cannot recreate an Attempt
or interpreter. It retains partial setup and transfers the pair plus admitted
runtime synchronously. Session adopts before phase/runtime rechecks. One pending
handoff fact plus exact-pair identity across retire's await prevents a late
replacement from being hidden by RETIRED. No new registry, lifecycle task or
distributed restart framework. Both replacement/final retirement use the same
helper preserving original cancellation and independent cleanup observations.

PredictionRecoveryFailed explicitly retains first prediction fault, later
replacement/cleanup failure and interruption cause. Production's existing bounded
protected notice owner projects/redacts first-fault evidence with actual Attempt
IDs, retaining failed-output evidence via its existing api_notice_failure field.
Successful recovery adds no unreserved traceback graph to scientific evidence.
Second failure or unconfirmed cleanup stays operational; there is no third try,
automatic Job replay or increased resource limit.

2026-10-23 native history release: source-grounded proposal, not implementation
============================================================================
Investigated at6dc5c1e. No native history, receipt, Attempt record or old
qualification artifact was changed or deleted. No model/API Job, native
prediction or new test run occurred. This is a proposed next bounded slice,
not closure of003 or qualification of unattended disk capacity.

Existing cutpoints and owners
-----------------------------
- tools/provider_workers.sh:217-249 owns exact engine removal, IPC cleanup and
  the final resource-report receipt. Its251-321 archive operation requires
  independently retired state plus exact consumed/result bytes, moves original
  owned/request trees and releases existing maps. That renews live slots only.
- nmrnet_provider/allocation.py:133-211 owns consumption after setup, host
  retirement and channel settlement. Its archived receipt is still being read
  asynchronously. Physical retirement or consumed publication alone grants no
  permission to delete that receipt or crash-reconciliation evidence.
- packages/provider-core/src/provider_core/controller.py:208-257 returns cleanly
  only after execution retirement, terminal publication and claim release.
  nmrnet_provider/session.py:424-443 confirms input/prediction owners, including
  replacement handoffs. A pending/failed cleanup never reaches that clean return.
- nmrnet_provider/service.py:282-308 reaches the existing next-work-cycle barrier
  after that return. admission.py:61-84 rejects every unresolved store neighbor,
  then archives each exact TerminalConfirmed record. attempt_files.py:335-380
  owns the archive's write/sync/unlink ordering. It does not own native files.
- TerminalConfirmed (attempt_record.py:237-246) establishes matching API receipt,
  NOT native retirement. Both preceding clean lifetime and durable archival
  must therefore precede a native release request; no one flag replaces them.

What remains necessary
----------------------
1. Active requests, in-flight setup/channel work, retired-but-unconsumed leaves,
   partial native archives and all uncertain transfer/cleanup evidence remain
   untouched. Native archive errors already hold maps and preserve remaining
   paths (provider_workers.sh:267-318). Neither API confirmation nor age repairs
   a native transfer that never completed.
2. Durable Attempt records retain original START/acknowledgement, execution-entry
   prepared identity, exact terminal body, protected scientific evidence and
   matching API receipt (attempt_record.py:122-136,182-205,237-246). These stay
   under AttemptStore ownership. Stopped recovery (nmrnet_provider/recovery.py:
   98-143) operates on those exact records, not native engine-command files.
   session.py:106-122,338-356 retains raw scientific output/diagnostics separately
   from its public rounding. Native history is not a substitute for that evidence.
3. Native history's owned/ subtree holds engine creation/posture/removal evidence
   and resource observations; requests/ holds allocation/retire/consumption bytes.
   These are valuable until the clean publication/archive cutpoint, especially
   after a controller crash. They are not required to replay a confirmed terminal
   command after that cutpoint. Input/artifact/source roots remain separate and
   must never be included in this deletion authority.
4. Existing receipts/history presence prevents old-token reuse before another
   create (provider_workers.sh:340-344). Erasing every token path would silently
   weaken that rule under today's random-token protocol. Compact archived
   receipts would preserve it, but still grow indefinitely. The bounded sequence
   alternative below preserves stale-request refusal without one file per token.
5. Scientific qualification capsules deliberately retain complete historical
   proof (tools/provider_service_qualification.sh:427-439). Existing capsules
   and that explicit qualification retention remain outside production pruning.

Smallest proposed change
------------------------
Use one awaited handoff between the existing service and host owners, immediately
AFTER the clean confirmed-inventory archival pass and BEFORE fresh selection.
The service authorizes release of prior completed work from THIS fresh host
lifetime; it supplies no path, shell command or new Job lifecycle state. The host
rechecks its own exact retired/consumed/archived evidence and no active or partial
native obligation before pruning completed leaves. Unknown names, symlinks,
mismatched receipts and partial transfers refuse release and retain the remaining
evidence. A copied diagnostics JSON or TerminalConfirmed flag alone never grants
deletion authority.

Closed-world prerequisite: provider_workers.sh:92-100 and provider.sh:606-616
refuse old/nonempty roots and require their exclusive locks. There is one serial
service/controller lifetime; all earlier controllers and setup/source/replacement
work must have settled, the WHOLE core inventory must be confirmed/archived, and
no fresh allocation passes the awaited release. This composes existing service
facts with host-owned retirement evidence. It does not generalize to simultaneous
lanes, an adopted old root, detached writers or a store with unresolved neighbors.
Under these premises a per-Attempt token list adds no necessary authority. The
initial stricter proposal and its supervisor attribution in4f9d81f are superseded
by this adjudication; do not reintroduce retired clients/tasks or another registry
to satisfy that earlier wording. A crash leaves the original installation for
stopped inspection, not automatic history reconstruction or deletion.

Carry the handoff through the existing private request/read-only-receipt grant,
with one outstanding operation and an exactly matched response identity. Keep
only its current request/receipt; do not accumulate a second history ledger.
The original asynchronous operation remains owned across cancellation/timeout.
No fresh admission passes until its outcome is known. A stop before publication
submits nothing; a stop after publication settles the original handoff. A partial
filesystem failure reports the original cause and removed-versus-retained facts,
holds further work and never reruns science or silently retries deletion. This
proposal does not extend native files' existing power-loss guarantees or add fsync.

Tests first in current focused allocation/service lanes: successful completion
and durable archive precede release; lost API ACK/archive error/unresolved neighbor
submit none; partial native archive and live/unconsumed worker refuse pruning;
old-token reuse still refuses after old receipts are gone; cancellation settles the same
handoff once; deletion/receipt failure preserves useful causes and prevents fresh
allocation. Keep existing scientific reference outputs and old capsules unchanged.

Adjudicated bounded native end state: one existing host generation and sequence
-----------------------------------------------------------------------------
The fresh, exclusively held installation already supplies the generation. No
extra generation service/file tree is required. Replace random allocation names
(native_factory.py:251-258) with increasing allocation numbers represented in the
existing32-lowercase-hex token shape. The factory chooses the next request number;
the host owns acceptance of exactly the next number before any create or explicit
never-dispatched retirement. Keep one accepted-number frontier plus existing
live/held maps. A request at/below that frontier whose original map is no longer
owned is stale even if its old history/receipts have been removed. Known current
maps still settle their ORIGINAL operations; old numbers never trigger a create.
Future gaps refuse rather than being skipped. Numeric range/overflow belongs to
these owners' actual representation, with explicit drain/refusal before wrap;
never reset the number inside a live installation. Token secrecy is not an
authority: private mounts, closed roles and fixed host grants already own it.

The clean service-wide handoff binds the expected accepted frontier. The host
requires exact completed dispositions for all retained work through that frontier
and no active/partial obligation. It can then release full native trees AND token
receipts, retaining only its current frontier and matched release response. A
stale release request cannot delete work after its declared frontier. One current
request/response is enough; there is no per-Job interval ledger. A host/controller
restart cannot silently reset and reuse the root: the existing nonempty-root
refusal remains. Never-dispatched requests, interrupted publications and missing
sequence entries need focused causal witnesses before this becomes code. This
is a proposed change to the production allocation pair, not to prestarted worker
grants or public API identifiers.
Here frontier is inclusive: the factory's largest issued number, consumed before
publication and never rolled back, must equal the host's largest accepted number.
An unseen reservation therefore refuses release rather than disappearing. The
host performs release inside its existing serialized request-service owner; any
request leaf, current/held map, partial transfer or frontier mismatch blocks it.

Confirmed Attempt retention: lifecycle obligation versus evidence policy
-----------------------------------------------------------------------
Already-confirmed recovery is a no-op: provider_core/recovery.py:98-115 returns
TerminalConfirmed unchanged, whereas TerminalPending delegates the exact stored
terminal command. Public receiving reads the API's result, not local history.
Thus no lifecycle replay obligation alone requires every confirmed byte forever.
The current history-key refusal (attempt_files.py:298-301) is also a local policy,
not the server's Job/Attempt admission authority. Normal discovery uses the API's
has_provider_execution_attempt=false and generates a fresh key (admission.py:
114-135); forever-retained old keys do not by themselves prevent another Job run.
Deleting them must not permit execution without the normal START acknowledgement
and durable execution-entry gate, or change unresolved recovery in any way.

There IS unique evidence: prepared identity, unrounded predictions, native/model
diagnostics and original API commands/receipts remain in the complete confirmed
record. A4-digit public result cannot reconstruct its full raw prediction. That
justifies an explicit scientific/operator retention window, not an undocumented
promise of infinite local storage. U19 (notes/013_sibling_practices_20261016.txt:
281-299) preserves evidence while a claim relies on it and debug captures until
resolution/reviewed replacement; it does not require every production record
forever. docs/provider.md:210-217 currently promises no automatic expiry; changing
that contract must be explicit. No existing byte is authorized for removal here.

Recommended healthy-run end state: one finite, operator-visible confirmed-record
window managed by the EXISTING AttemptStore/archive I/O owner, plus the bounded
native sequence/release above. Retain whole exact confirmed records in that
window rather than inventing a summary/second ledger. A finite record count and
the existing record-size cap yield a finite logical storage bound; reserve space
for active obligations and the archive transition. Expire only confirmed history
past that window after the clean cutpoint, with ordinary existing failure/poison/
settlement semantics. Never expire active, held or uncertain records. Published
qualification capsules and explicitly selected scientific evidence are outside
the production window. Past expiry, the API still owns its public result; local
raw-value and historical diagnostic lookup is no longer promised. State that limit
plainly. No retention count/default or migration deletion is approved by this
investigation. Without a finite evidence window, full disk closure is unproven
even if native history becomes bounded; bulk pruning alone is not that closure.

No quota framework, offload service, extra registry or fsync campaign is proposed.
Resource bounds are for healthy operation; failures still hold original evidence
and require exceptional reconciliation instead of silently discarding it. Actual
normal GUI submission and real-model semantic proof in003R45/R50 remain pending.
Independent read-only source review confirmed the existing cutpoints, consumption
receipt reader and held-transfer protection. It identified random token generation,
never-dispatched sequence holes and unique private scientific evidence as required
design considerations. The supervisor retracted the earlier per-Attempt-binding
requirement after reviewing fresh/exclusive root and serial lifetime premises.
Final independent review found no causal contradiction under those premises and
required explicit treatment of never-dispatched reservations/frontier mismatch.
This is design evidence only; sequence allocation, expiry and pruning remain
unimplemented and unqualified.

Native sequence implementation, 2026-10-23
------------------------------------------
NativeAttemptFactory consumes an increasing positive signed-64-bit number before
request construction, encoded as the existing32-lowercase-hex allocation token.
The original fresh/exclusive host owner accepts exactly its next number before
dispatch or never-dispatched retirement. Gaps, zero, overflow and stale numbers
refuse without engine effects; neither side wraps or reuses a number. Incomplete
publication consumes no host number until an exact allocation or retirement is
present. Existing maps/receipt checks and uncertain setup ownership are retained.
The format qualification alone carries its one host's scalar between separately
scoped case factories; production has one factory per fresh host lifetime.

lanes then passed native-factory14/0.045s and allocation53/19.408s, including the
existing real client/recording host protocol. Independent read-only code/caller
review, pinned Ruff0.15.15, bash syntax and diff checks passed. Only disposable
test roots were used; no scientific matrix, external call, store or capsule was
changed. Native release and confirmed-history expiry remain the next slices;
this sequence-only change does not authorize or perform their deletion.

Confirmed evidence window and shutdown batch, 2026-10-23
-------------------------------------------------------
Required StoreLimits keyword/TOML field confirmed_history_records now names a
positive finite record count; the authored configuration example is64, not an
implicit default. Existing callers supply their policy explicitly. H is the
confirmed window, A the active-slot limit and R the full record budget. Steady
history is at most H records; the bounded archive transition permits H+A, plus
one complete staging/archive copy. Production R=3,725,916 from
provider_core/attempt_record.py::MAX_RECORD_BYTES gives H64/A2 peak249,636,372
bytes (~238MiB), excluding filesystem metadata and configured reserve. Existing
fstatvfs headroom still covers outstanding growth/staging; it reserves no blocks
and cannot constrain other writers or guarantee failure-free disk operation.

AttemptFiles.open bounds history NAMES at H+A for stopped inspection, preserving
original active/matching-history admission. It does not decode unrelated old
payloads merely to inspect/recover an active obligation. Ordinary service
preflight/fresh admission require <=H before any archive/release. Existing excess
history refuses rather than migrating. AttemptFiles.trim_confirmed_history owns
the whole-byte admission at the deletion boundary: active inventory must be
empty; every historical file must pass original private-file/TerminalConfirmed
admission BEFORE any unlink. It reads one payload at a time, explicitly discarding
per-record locals after saving only (receipt.committed_at, canonical filename).
Sorted keys select oldest whole records; retained bytes are never regenerated.
History directory fsync follows observed unlink operations. AttemptStore's
original executor/future retains cancelled or expired I/O without resubmission.

The earlier per-phase stop rule is explicitly SUPERSEDED for an admitted
maintenance batch. Its counterexample: stop during archival at a full H window
could leave H+1, then the next ordinary startup would refuse its own valid
transition. One existing-service-owned maintenance task now spans complete
archive -> native-release ACK -> trim. Stop before the complete inventory gate
admits none; afterward shutdown drains/settles that SAME task, including startup
and between Jobs. Bounded timeout/cancellation retains it and its native/storage
dependencies; no replacement cleanup, hidden cap exception, restart migration or
durable marker/ledger is added. Genuine failed/crashed maintenance still holds
remaining evidence for explicit inspection; old native roots are not adopted.

StoreFailed retains expiry phase, exact observed removed filenames, uncertain
target, unconfirmed directory sync, original cause and existing cleanup causes.
provider_core.failure_projection projects only these owned facts; NMRNet's
actual stderr receiving renderer preserves errno/phase/removal facts and key
redaction. Failed sync or partial deletion poisons further storage use, with no
automatic expiry retry. Ordinary Docker logs remain bounded as described in019.

finishes a third Job with exactly three starts/model/scientific executions.
The older lost-reply/retirement/headroom gates remain; its reusable fixture uses
